Goodyear Tire CEO Mark Stewart steps down as company continues turnaround plan

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Goodyear Tire & Rubber Co. announced Monday that CEO Mark Stewart would be stepping down from his role effective immediately.

Board member Roger Wood is taking over as interim CEO and president, Goodyear said.

The leadership change comes as the tire company is in the midst of a multiyear transformation plan.

Stewart was hired in January 2024 to lead the company, with the goal of revitalizing the legacy American business to make it more efficient and profitable.

The company has faced disappointing results thus far, reporting last week third-quarter results that saw net sales down 2.3% year over year and an adjusted net loss of $31 million. Goodyear failed to reach a 10% operating margin by the end of last year, which was a major goal of the turnaround plan.

Goodyear said it formed a special committee of the board earlier this year to conduct a "strategic review of the company's businesses and operations focused on identifying opportunities to strengthen performance and drive shareholder value."

It said Monday it will continue that work by evaluating strategic and operational initiatives that include further optimizing the company's business mix and footprint, improving efficiency and investing in new products and capabilities that support long-term growth.

"Goodyear has made meaningful progress in becoming a more efficient and focused company, and the board believes this is the right moment to transition leadership to accelerate that progress and capture additional opportunity," Goodyear Chair Joe Hinrichs said in a press release.

The company has faced headwinds from tariffs, higher raw material costs and increased competition from China.

Wood has been a member of the company's board of directors since July 2023 and was previously the co-CEO of Tenneco as well as CEO of Dana Holding Corporation.

The company will start a search for a permanent replacement as part of its strategic review.

Shares of Goodyear were down roughly 4% in early trading Monday.

Apple pushing faster on agentic Siri would carry three big risks

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Yesterday saw two reports about Apple software development. The first said that the company was aiming to give Siri more agentic capabilities “as soon as next year,” while the second said that CEO John Ternus is pushing Craig Federighi to speed up software development.

If accurate, it seems near-certain the two reports are connected. But if Ternus really is pushing for an acceleration in Siri’s ability to act as an AI agent, that comes with three potentially huge risks …

Both reports were by Bloomberg’s Mark Gurman, though he didn’t directly connect them. On the Siri side, his newsletter contrasted the agentic abilities of some competitor models, suggesting that Apple ultimately wants to achieve the same things.

Dots, Muse and Grok can keep track of goals, determine what needs to happen next and continue pursuing objectives without the user initiating every individual step. Apple ultimately wants to go in this direction, I’m told, and I believe the company could make Siri more like an agent as early as next year.

The report about Ternus pushing Federighi for faster software development mentions “moving beyond necessary improvements to Siri,” of which agentic capabilities would be the obvious candidate.

But agentic capabilities are among the most dangerous forms of AI given the multitude of things that could go very, very wrong.

The most obvious is simply failing to carry out the task. Depending on the importance of it, the consequences could range from annoyance through to significant harm.

Technology has long failed to live up to one of its biggest promises: relieving us of mundane work in order to devote our time to more fulfilling things. For example, obtaining tickets to a popular gig or other event, which currently involves scheduling time in order to repeatedly refresh a page, perhaps wait through a queuing system, and finally carry out the transaction. It would be great to be able to hand this off to an AI.

But even on something relatively unimportant like this, if we delegated the task and the AI failed to obtain the tickets, especially if this was due to a silly error, this could prove enormously frustrating. If the task were more important, the results could obviously be far worse.

Potentially even worse is achieving the task but doing so in inappropriate ways. We’ve already seen high-profile examples of this, like the Hugging Face hack caused by an AI model seeking so hard to carry out instructions that it used underhand methods to bypass crucial safeguards in the process.

Another huge risk is to privacy. Just today we learned that Claude Code exposed over 2,000 real secrets to GitHub, as Cybernews reports.

Over two thousand real secrets, including passwords, API tokens, SSH root logins, and even admin-level cloud keys, were found leaking on GitHub after developers clicked “Yes, and don’t ask again” in Claude Code […] When a developer grants Claude Code permission to proceed with “Yes, and don’t ask again,” it saves the exact command, including secrets, to a file, .claude/settings.local.json, inside the project folder. This file should never be exposed publicly [but] Apiiro, an application security company, found 104,185 public copies of such files on GitHub across 103,617 repositories.

This was just a few days after Anthropic started encouraging Claude users to connect their bank and finance card accounts to the AI chatbot. (My own answer is “Hell, no.”)

When we delegate a task to an AI system that requires access to any of our personal data, and we’re not expected to check on it again until the task is complete, the potential for disaster here is enormous.

It’s worth prefixing this with a note that the reliability of the paired reports is far from certain. In particular, in a closed-door meeting between Ternus and Federighi, it’s hard to see how the content could have leaked unless one of the two men chose to do this. If Ternus leaked it, that suggests a very high level of frustration on his part. But leaving that aside …

We’re currently witnessing an AI arms race, with each company aiming to beat its competitors to next-generation capabilities, even while acknowledging a non-trivial possibility that the pace of development could put humanity’s future at risk.

In my view, the very last thing we need is Apple joining this race. I know it can be frustrating when Apple lags behind other companies in the introduction of some types of new tech, but this is one field in which I would be very happy to see Apple proceed very much more cautiously than others.

Right now, AI needs at least one player that prioritizes safety and privacy over speed to market – in that way, we’d get a choice. If we want agentic capabilities sooner rather than later, and are prepared to run the risks involved in this, then we can turn to companies like Anthropic and Meta. If we are prepared to wait for safer systems, then Apple should provide that alternative.

What’s your view on this? Please share your thoughts in the comments.

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Good morning, everyone, and welcome to another working week. We hope the weekend respite was relaxing and invigorating, because the usual routine of online meetings, phone calls, and deadlines has returned. You knew this would happen, yes? So to cope, we are firing up the trusty kettle and brewing another cuppa stimulation. Our choice today is blueberry hibiscus — with a dollop of bourbon honey. Please feel free to join us. Or grab a bottle of water, if you prefer. Meanwhile, we have assembled the latest line-up of interesting items for you to peruse. We hope your journey today is meaningful and productive. And as always, please keep in touch. Your tips and insights help the world go around. …

Novartis is largely abandoning an experimental drug analysts once considered a potential blockbuster after a study found it had little promise for preventing heart attacks and cardiovascular deaths, Bloomberg News notes. The move came as details of a key Novartis study were presented at the American Heart Association meeting this weekend. The growing field of research aimed at lipoprotein(a), a genetic risk factor for cardiovascular disease carried by 1 in 5 people, took a hit earlier this year when top-line Novartis results showed an experimental drug reduced Lp(a) but failed to lower serious outcomes. Other drugmakers pursuing Lp(a) drugs include Amgen, Eli Lilly, and Silence Therapeutics, STAT adds.

The Biotechnology Innovation Organization is joining the legal fight against a Trump administration pilot program aimed at aligning drug prices in Medicare with prices charged in peer countries, STAT says. BIO and two California-based biotech trade groups filed a complaint in the U.S. District Court for the Northern District of California asserting the Global Benchmark for Efficient Drug Pricing demonstration, known as GLOBE, oversteps Medicare’s statutory authority. The complaint seeks to halt the model. GLOBE, which applies to Medicare Part B drugs administered in doctor offices, is not expected to have much impact because the administration exempted all but a handful of companies in return for their agreement to voluntary deals to charge Medicaid most-favored-nation prices.

Russell Brand Sentenced to Over 10 Years in Prison for Rape and Sexual Assault

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

"I just hope he is not allowed to hurt any more women," said one of Brand's victims from Southwark Crown Court.

Russell Brand has been sentenced to 10 years and six months in prison after being found guilty of rape and sexual assault.

He’ll be expected to serve around seven years of that prison sentence before being released on licence. Brand was silent as the sentencing was read out and mouthed “I love you” to his wife, Laura, as he was led away.

The judge, Justice Bennathan, took Brand through all of his crimes in extensive detail, citing the witness impact statements from the three victims before handing down the sentence. He said Brand operated with “staggering arrogance” during the attacks and that his entourage often “indulged” his “dreadful behavior.” He said toward the end of his statement: “Whatever women wear, wherever they go, indeed whatever they drink, they retain the right to say ‘no’ and to demand justice from criminal courts when sexual predators like you, Russell Brand, act without their consent.” Related Stories Movies 'A Photographer in Cannes' Follows an Estonian Who Knows the World's Red (and Black) Carpets - and Loved Getting a Vin Diesel Fist Bump Movies THR Visionary: 5 Janusz Kaminski Shots

After a four-week trial at Southwark Crown Court, the jury delivered the verdicts on Thursday, finding the disgraced comedian and presenter guilty on one count of rape and three counts of sexual assault. He was found not guilty on two other rape charges and not guilty of attempted indecent assault.

The Met Police has also confirmed Brand is under investigation over further rape allegations from women not connected to the trial.

The victims, who have been granted lifelong anonymity, spoke from behind a curtain at the sentencing hearing. “I wanted to express how this crime has impacted me,” said one during her witness impact statement according to BBC News coverage. “I’ve been in shock and turmoil for weeks. I was extremely upset and traumatised. I didn’t have any respect for myself… I was thinking I was damaged goods — scared of giving off signals that could be taken the wrong way.” She continued that there were many times she felt “physically sick” seeing Brand in the media. “I just want to say, a leopard may change its spots, but it’s still a leopard,” she added.

Another victim said in court on Monday: “Russell Brand is a sexual predator who has no remorse. […] I just hope he is not allowed to hurt any more women.”

But the star’s defence argued that he is “a fundamentally different person today” and said his wife is experiencing “total devastation” from the convictions. Journalists inside the court noted Laura Brand was shaking her head as one victim read her witness impact statement. Brand’s lawyer, Ian Winter, said the couple is “wrestling” with how to explain to one of their children, aged 10, what has happened.

The trial — and verdicts — captivated the country, leaving not just the industry but the entire British public with questions over how Brand went unreported for so long.

Thursday’s guilty rape verdict relates to a woman who met Brand at a Hammersmith Apollo gig of his in 2009. “I was expecting to have sex with him,” the victim told police about going to Brand’s house a week later. After consensual vaginal sex, Brand turned her onto her front and anally raped her. She recalled feeling as though “she couldn’t speak or breathe” and said she was in pain. When finally able to speak, the woman told Brand to stop “a few times quietly and then really loudly.” The anal rape lasted “minutes,” and Brand “looked absolutely fucking horrified” when it was over, she said. She expected him to apologize, but he only told her: “You need to clear up the mess you made.”

Brand was also found guilty of three sexual assaults. One was his grabbing a victim’s breasts at a bar in Soho in 2004; another was an incident in 2004/2005 at Capital Radio where he pushed a victim up against a wall, kissing her and grabbing at her body, breasts, sides and buttocks “aggressively”; and lastly, an incident where he put his head and nose in the “vaginal area” of a female colleague in 2009.

Russell Brand Sentenced to 10 Years in Prison After Rape, Sexual Assault Convictions

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Russell Brand has been sentenced to 10 years and six months in prison after being convicted of rape and sexual assault in his London criminal trial last week.

He was facing a potential sentence of 19 years to life in prison for the rape conviction. The maximum sentence for sexual assault is 10 years.

In relation to the sexual assaults, Judge Bennathan handed down a sentence of nine months for Count 3, 18 months for Count 5 (reduced from two years) and eight months for Count 7, which would be served concurrently. He also imposed a sentence of nine years imprisonment for the Count 6 charge of rape. The judge said Brand must serve two thirds of the sentence before he can be released on licence. Related Stories NFL to Face New Trial in Sunday Ticket Class-Action Lawsuit to Determine Financial Damages

The former actor and comedian was found guilty Thursday on three counts of sexual assault and one count of rape. He was found not guilty of two other rape charges and a count of attempted indecent assault.

At the sentencing hearing on Monday morning, the women in the case told the judge of the ongoing impact Brand’s crimes had on them. One victim, who was sexually assaulted at a radio station in 2005 (Count 5), said in a statement read by the Crown Prosecution Service’s lawyer the attack had had a “lasting impact” on her life.

The victim of Count 3, who was sexually assaulted in a Soho bar in 2004 said that after the attack it was “impossible to escape seeing him all over the media, being celebrated for his disgusting behaviour against women like it was all a joke” while the victim of Count 7, who was assaulted by Brand during a shoot for Channel 4 in 2009, said she “lost trust in people and felt that my working environment wasn’t safe” in the aftermath. She also said the incident was “brushed under the carpet by senior management.”

The victim of Brand’s most serious crime, who was anally raped by Brand outside home in Hampstead before he ordered her to change the sheets and had her escorted out by his driver (Count 6), said she feared she had contracted HIV after the encounter. The judge said that Brand removing the condom and ejaculating inside the victim without her consent were aggravating factors in his sentencing decision. “The whole episode reeks of casual disrespect and your belief that fame meant you could act how you liked, when you liked, unhindered by the rules by which decent people live,” said the judge.

“About 20 years ago, Russell Brand, you were becoming famous,” Judge Bennathan began his sentencing remarks. “You were clever and, in many people’s eyes, funny and charismatic. You had also become a sexual predator.”

Of the radio station sexual assault, in which he forcibly kissed the victim, pushed her against the wall and ran his hands all over her body after encountering her in the corridor, the judge said: “No doubt your companions thought it was outrageous laddish behaviour that only you could get away with: It was outrageous and possibly laddish, but also a criminal sexual offence, and as it turns out you haven’t got away with it.”

Brand, who stood as the sentence was handed down, did not react although his wife Laura Gallacher clasped her hands and was supported by friends. Brand’s father Ronald was also in the gallery to watch his son’s sentencing.

Brand’s sentence began on Thursday when he was taken into police custody after the judge refused to grant him conditional bail following the jury’s verdict. As Brand was taken to the cells, he mouthed “I’m OK” and “I’m good” to his lawyers and gave a thumbs-up sign, according to Sky News.

Brand did not visibly react when the verdicts were read out. His wife, Laura Gallacher, rushed out of the courtroom when Brand was taken to the cells to wait before his bail application.

Brand was accused of raping three women at various locations between 1999 and 2009. One of the alleged victims also accused him of sexual assault in addition to oral rape. He was also accused of one count of attempted indecent assault at MTV’s U.K. offices in 2001 and one count of sexual assault in 2009. After the trial concluded, the Metropolitan Police confirmed that he is being investigated on further allegations of rape from two other women.

Allegations against Brand went public in 2023 with a docuseries from the Sunday Times and Channel 4’s “Dispatches.” Before then, he was best known as a comedian and for his roles in films like “Forgetting Sarah Marshall” and “Get Him to the Greek.”

Chelsea transfer news: Blues ‘ready to approve’ January move for £17m ace amid Celtic interest

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

By Oliver Thomas | 09 Nov 2026 14:23

Scottish giants Celtic and a number of Championship sides have reportedly been given fresh hope in their pursuit of Chelsea starlet Kendry Paez.

Paez agreed to join Chelsea from Ecuadorian outfit Independiente del Valle in 2023 before his £17m transfer to the Premier League club was made official in the summer of 2025 after his 18th birthday.

The attacking midfielder experienced a disappointing loan spell at Chelsea’s sister club Strasbourg in France, before his January loan to River Plate was cut short in August due to a lack of regular game time.

Paez is now back at Chelsea but does not feature in Xabi Alonso’s first-team plans, with the youngster currently training and representing the club’s Under-21 side.

According to TEAMtalk, Chelsea are now exploring the possibility of finding Paez a ‘new opportunity’ in January, actively looking for loan options for the second half of the season.

The report adds that any loan agreement for Paez must guarantee regular first-team football for the youngster, as Chelsea still have very high hopes for the 27-cap Ecuador international.

Interest in Paez is said to have emerged from multiple leagues, with Celtic among the clubs who had previously been alerted to his availability towards the end of the summer window.

Martin O'Neill’s side are said to remain interest in Paez and may be able to offer the youngster European football, should they sneak into the top 24 in the Europa League standings and secure at least a knockout round playoff spot.

Celtic also sit top of the Scottish Premiership table as they bid to defend their title, but they are set to face stern competition from Championship teams for Paez, including current leaders Birmingham City and mid-table outfit Blackburn Rovers.

It is claimed that Blackburn were offered the chance to take Paez on loan in the summer and may get that opportunity again in January, while Birmingham are said to be prepared to consider a loan deal as they fight for Premier League promotion.

In addition, TEAMtalk claims that Chelsea have reached out to three further Championship clubs over a move for Paez, seemingly complicating Celtic’s pursuit of the teenager.

Turkish giants Trabzonspor are another team said to be monitoring his situation, while Belgian side Mechelen allegedly contacted Chelsea in the summer and may reignite their interest in Paez, who is viewed as a potential No.10 or right-sided option.

How John Malkovich Nailed the Role of His Career with ‘Wild Horse Nine’

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Editor’s Note: This interview was originally published during the 2026 Telluride Film Festival. Searchlight Pictures opened “Wild Horse Nine” in select theaters on November 6.

While early “Wild Horse Nine” press screenings in Los Angeles and New York got the ball rolling, the first whiff of a strong public reaction was the Venice Film Festival, where edgy auteur Martin McDonagh, John Malkovich, and Sam Rockwell soaked up sustained applause. Then they were whisked to the Colorado Rockies to screen the movie in Telluride.

Instantly, Malkovich jumped up the Best Actor rankings for what would be his third Oscar nomination. His first two were for Supporting Actor, in 1985 for “Places in the Heart,” and 1993 for “In the Line of Fire.” Now the movie is screening at the Toronto International Film Festival ahead of its November 6 release. Related Stories ‘Half Nelson’ at 20: How a $700,000 Indie Addiction Drama Earned Ryan Gosling His First Oscar Nod Cinematographers’ Favorite Festival Is All About AI This Year — but Artists Still Hate the Tech

I caught up with Malkovich in the back of a large black SUV parked in front of the Herzog Theatre before his first of two Telluride Tributes.

The following interview has been edited and condensed for length and clarity.

Anne Thompson: How did you first get to know Martin McDonagh?

John Malkovich: I was offered a couple things of Martin’s over the years. One, “Seven Psychopaths,” one, [the play] “A Behanding in Spokane,” but I couldn’t do it. I was busy. I love the scripts, but I couldn’t do it, and so then this came. I thought the thing was great. Beautiful piece of writing, just a perfect screenplay, and I couldn’t believe I got sent it. I couldn’t believe he asked me to do it.

Yeah. So I said, “Yeah!” with no hesitation at all. I have two little granddaughters. I don’t love being away for two, three months, whatever. But it’s part of the job, so you got to do it. We met in London because I’d never met Martin. We rehearsed five to six days in London with Sam, who I love. I’d worked with him before on “The Hitchhiker’s Guide to the Galaxy,” not a long gig, but I knew Sam a bit socially. And I’d worked with Buscemi before twice because he was in our film “Ghost World,” and we worked together in “Con Air.” Sam and I hit it off, workwise, very well, very quickly.

You may not agree, but I think Sam receded slightly and supported you in an amazing way. I find it moving, actually, that he did this for you. Do you feel that’s what he did?

Yes and no. I think he played his part. And the part [of Lee] is slightly recessive in that sense. Chris is the older one. He’s the wild card. He’s killed whatever, 168 people, but apparently none while they were eating. And he’s a legend in the CIA. Sam is an exceptionally generous actor, as generous as you could ever find, you couldn’t ask for a better partner in every way.

You’ve been doing this a long time. Your first Oscar nomination was in 1985. That’s over 40 years ago. Both your nominations have been for Supporting Actor. Are you a character actor?

I’ve had leading roles. But people don’t understand what a role is, the general public or anybody except actors. First of all, you can be a terrific actor in anything, but if the film isn’t good, it’s utterly meaningless. You may as well not have bothered. You may as well not have done it. It’s a waste of time. For a good role to be really good, people have to not just like the way you do that, but like what the character is doing, and understand it if they don’t like it, and they have to like the arc of the character. The best performances I gave would have been on stage first of all, but secondly, those in the cinema wouldn’t necessarily be in good movies, ugh. And that, on a certain level, has precious little to do with performance X, Y, Z. That’s just one small part of a movie.

In this case, you’re given an extraordinary amount of verbiage. So you had to make the rhythm of it, the sense of it, the emotion of it. Sometimes it’s hilariously funny, sometimes sad, and you run that gamut.

Yeah, but that’s great writing. This material, I believe, causes a big wave, and you ride that wave where it’s going, through comedy, through drama, through tragedy, even if that’s the nature of a particular text, and that’s what actors do. The collision between the public and the material is caused by a recognition in their lives.

Our CIA agent Chris is messing around with Chile in 1973 as Pinochet waits in the wings.

November 22, 1963 is a trauma, as is September 11, 2001, and 1973 in Chile. For those of us who were alive at that time, it’s immutable. Doesn’t matter your politics then or now, or if you had any. Doesn’t matter anything. I directed Don DeLillo’s great novel “Libra,” adapted it for the stage with Don, and Don referred to it in the book as the seven seconds that broke the back of the American century in Chile and it was not the only place on earth: Chile, Argentina, Brazil, Peru, Ecuador.

And various actors have been bad along with us, and possibly not worse, but the birthday party was spoiled. What’s left, and it caused generations of pain, of nihilism, of loss of innocence, of loss of faith in a political system. People everywhere can understand that, and Martin mines that beautifully. It’s beautifully structured in the writing and modulated in the filmmaking. So the wave is there.

Your character is fun to watch because he’s unfettered, he’s unleashed, he doesn’t give a fuck anymore. He’s just going to say what he wants to say, and he’s going to tell the truth.

And people appreciate that because in this culture, it’s very odd. You say this word or that word, and the anti-social media mob goes berserk. I don’t have social media. I don’t want it designated to me in the slightest. I don’t care what they say. They can say whatever they want, and so can people who offend you. They can say whatever they want, and voila, who cares? And this idea that you can’t say that because it’s offensive to this or that, and covers everything and every topic and every subject and every blah blah blah seems incredibly dumb. It’s a dumb world.

Well, that’s one of the great things about being a certain age, right? You don’t care anymore. He doesn’t, and maybe you don’t.

I stay off [social media] because I don’t see the value. I’m doing things. If people like them, beautiful. If they hate them, beautiful. I do a [stage and short film] piece called “The Music Critic,” which is all the worst reviews of all the most beautiful pieces of classical music in the world. They were all trashed. They were all hated. So what? Get used to it. Grow a thick skin, go on with your life, do what you think is good.

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Blackburn Rovers and Birmingham City are both keen on a deal to sign Kendry Paez from Chelsea.

According to Teamtalk, the two Championship sides are both keen on the 19-year-old ahead of the January transfer window.

The teenager, who has been tipped to potentially be 'better than Messi' by ex-Ecuador international Carlos Tenorio, is currently a part of the first team squad at Stamford Bridge after returning early from a loan spell with River Plate.

However, he is not a part of Xabi Alonso’s plans at the moment, and has yet to play a single minute for the Spaniard this season.

The attacking-midfielder could be available in the new year, with the Premier League side seeking a loan move in order for Paez to gain valuable experience.

Chelsea signed the Ecuadorian in 2025 in a deal worth £17.3 million, but he has yet to make his debut for the club.

It’s believed that both Birmingham and Blackburn are keen on a deal to sign Paez when the winter market opens in January.

However, the pair will face competition from Scottish giants Celtic, who are also interested in signing the 19-year-old.

The playmaker previously spent time on loan with Strasbourg, with the French club owned by the same group as the Blues.

Paez wasn’t ready for top flight football in France last year, and his underwhelming performances reflected that.

But he’s now had time with Chelsea’s underage team to develop his game and better prepare for senior football, as he is still only 19 and moving from South America to Europe isn’t an easy transition.

Donnie Dunagan, Original Voice of Bambi, Dies at 92

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

The future U.S. Marine also appeared as a youngster in the horror films ‘Son of Frankenstein’ and ‘Tower of London’ during his brief stay in Hollywood.

Donnie Dunagan, who provided the voice of Bambi for the original Disney classic and acted alongside Basil Rathbone and Boris Karloff in two films before being awarded three Purple Hearts as a U.S. Marine, has died. He was 92.

Dunagan died Wednesday of lung cancer, author and film historian Tom Weaver reported.

The blond, curly haired Dunagan had added a dose of sweetness to the 1939 Universal horror films Son of Frankenstein and Tower of London when his mom answered a phone call from none other than Walt Disney himself at the family’s home in Westwood. Related Stories TV 'Ellen DeGeneres Show' Producer Matthew Wright's Cause of Death Revealed Movies Paul Howard, Son of Moe Howard of The Three Stooges, Dies at 91

The studio chief wanted the kid to “help his artist put a child’s expression on a deer,” Dunagan recalled in a 2025 interview, so he and his mom drove to the new Disney lot in Burbank.

Before long, Dunagan was put to work on Bambi (1942), and he would spend more than a year on the animated film. In one of the movie’s most emotional sequences, his white-tailed character cries out for his mother after they are chased through the woods by a hunter.

“I remember this well,” he said in 2011. “When I was told to say, with some stress, ‘Mother … Mother,’ I must have not had the tone of fear that the story needed. A coach, I think it was a nice lady at the studio, asked me how I would cry out loud if my own real mother was lost and in great danger. That made it easy.”

Bambi would be his seventh and last film.

During his quarter-century in the military, Dunagan said he never told anyone about his four years in Hollywood. “One of the biggest regrets of my life is that I didn’t have the courage to tell my close personal friends in the Marine Corps that I had been a part of the wonderful work of Mr. Disney,” he said.

Donald Roan Dunagan was born in San Antonio on Aug. 16, 1934. He moved with his parents, Warren and Louise, to Memphis, and when he was 3, he tap-danced in a talent contest, winning the $100 prize, huge money in those days. More importantly, an RKO talent scout was in the audience, and he arranged for the Dunigans to travel to Los Angeles by train.

After an audition, the youngster made his big-screen debut in Mother Carey’s Chickens (1938), directed by Rowland V. Lee and starring Anne Shirley, Ruby Keeler and Walter Brennan.

Lee then cast him as Peter von Frankenstein, the son of Rathbone’s Baron Wolf von Frankenstein, in the second sequel to James Whale’s 1931 classic. During production, he often played checkers with Karloff, who was in full makeup and dressed as the monster. (He said he won four bits off him once.)

In Lee’s Tower of London, Dunagan’s Baby Prince and Lady Mowbray (played by 7-year-old Joan Carroll) are wed in an arranged marriage ceremony in the St. John Chapel. Because of budget concerns, the comical scene nearly was excised from the movie.

“I think Basil Rathbone was responsible for me being in Tower of London,” he recalled in a 2004 interview. “He was angry that they kept cutting scenes out of the script. Not angry like rednecks get angry — he was a gentleman, always — but he was disturbed.”

Dunagan also appeared in The Forgotten Woman in 1939, then showed up in Vigil in the Night (1940), directed by George Stevens, and Meet the Chump (1941) while working on Bambi through early 1941. (Bambi as an adolescent was voiced by Hardie Albright.)

Dunagan said that the hardest part about Bambi “was being serious. I loved the studio. The people were very different. I had a great time. The animators and all the different people at Disney offered to show me how certain things worked, and the ice cream in the dining room was great.”

He said that original drawings for the film had Bambi’s mom killed on camera, but Walt Disney “had the very good taste to direct that to be changed.” He also noted that the film’s release was delayed months because of Pearl Harbor and that the studio received lots of hate mail from hunters, who complained that the film “traumatized” the shooting of deer.

After Bambi, Dunagan’s parents divorced. He lived in several boarding homes, boxed and played football in high school and supported himself before joining the Marines in 1952 at age 18. He said he became the youngest drill instructor in their history and served three tours in Vietnam before retiring as a major in 1977.

Dunagan was a counterintelligence officer and a Force Recon Commander, and in addition to his three Purple Hearts, he was awarded a Bronze Star, a Navy and Marine Corps Commendation Medal with V device, an Army Commendation Medal and two Navy and Marine Corps Achievement Medals.

He kept his Bambi gig a secret back then because he had “a dislike for showoffs and those that boasted about themselves.” He also didn’t want anyone mocking him or calling him “Major Bambi.”

After the service, Dunagan worked for financial companies in New York, Los Angeles and San Antonio and as a CEO of the Pace Corp. in L.A.

When the Disney company learned he was still alive, he was asked to participate in a DVD release of Bambi, and he spoke at conventions and festivals and as an ambassador for the company for years.

Survivors include his wife, Dana, whom he married in September 1992.

“I could be working in the White House and children could care less,” he said. “But let someone say, ‘That dude over there was the face or voice of Bambi,’ and I am an immediate adopted grandfather to them. That is just an unmatchable joy and a real responsibility.”

Justin Timberlake Makes Rare Public Appearance With Sons and Niece on Runway at Down Syndrome Foundation Fashion Show

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

The singer walked with niece Zaya at Saturday's (Nov. 7) 19th annual fashion show in Denver.

Justin Timberlake made a rare public appearance with his two young sons, 11-year-old Silas and 6-year-old Phineas, as the family gathered in Denver on Saturday (Nov.7) to help celebrate the Global Down Syndrome Foundation’s 19th annual fashion show.

According to People magazine, Timberlake walked the runway with his 7-year-old niece, Zaya, while attending the nonprofit’s Be Beautiful Be Yourself fashion show in the Mile High city in support of brother-in-law Justin Biel and his wife Rose’s daughter, who has Down syndrome.

“Jessica and I adore Zaya, and we couldn’t be prouder of the remarkable difference she is making in the world,” Timberlake said in a statement shared with the organization, the magazine reported. “Every person with Down syndrome deserves the opportunity to reach their full potential, and that’s why GLOBAL’s work means so much to our family.”

In an Instagram Story featuring a picture of the singer, his sons and Zaya, he wrote, “We walked my beautiful niece Zaya down the @globaldownsyndrome runway last night ❤️.” He followed that with a slide in which he held a smiling Zaya in his arms on the red carpet, with the caption “Proud uncle!”

In photos from the night, Zaya held her uncle and cousin Silas’ hands as she walked down the catwalk with a big smile. The Timberlake men came dressed to impress, with Justin rocking a roomy black suit, while Phineas matched his dad’s energy in all black and Silas opted for black pants with a black leather jacket over a white shirt and black tie, accented by sunglasses. Zaya made her own statement in a black-and-white polka dot cress and fringe boots.

In a video from the event, JT and Silas bust some moves as Zaya gets a boost up onto the singer’s hip as they wave to the crowd. “She has some moves. Maybe her uncle Justin has taught her some moves and her auntie Jessica,” says the show’s announcer to cheers from the audience.

The organization teased the appearance last week, posting a pic of Timberlake and wife Jessica Biel with her brother and his wife, writing, “🎉 BIG NEWS! Grammy and Emmy Award-winning superstar @justintimberlake will rock the runway with his beautiful niece, #GLOBALAmbassador alum Zaya Biel @lovelyzayab, at our 19th annual #BeBeautifulBeYourself Fashion Show on Saturday, November 7th in Denver! 💖”

In 2024, Jessica Biel walked the carpet with Zaya, who was that year’s Global Ambassador for the organization and she also attended in honor of Zaya last year and has shared photos with her niece on World Down Syndrome Day as well.

This Traditional Archer Tagged a Big 8-Pointer With a Longbow and Stone Point Arrowhead He Made Himself

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 9, 2026 2:20 PM EST

Get everything from whitetail news and opinion pieces, to advanced hunting tactics and cutting-edge gear reviews.

It was the evening of October 25 when Joe McConnell, a traditional bowhunter from central Ohio, climbed into a ladder stand with a backdrop of heavy cover.

McConnell was hunting with a longbow in a narrow strip of woods that he owns. The spot was overlooking a travel lane deer were using to enter a large field of standing corn.

“I was really after a nice 10-point buck I had on trail cameras,” McConnell tells Outdoor Life. “The wind was perfect that evening, blowing right in my face. I was looking forward to filling my first landowner tag with a deer on my own property.”

“I’d made a mock scrape in front of my stand, with a licking branch above it about 12 yards from my ladder,” says the 38-year-old archer. “I heard him in the corn about six rows in, and he came to check the scrape.

“I saw his rack above the standing corn field coming to me, and when he stopped broadside quartering away at 12 yards, I took my shot.”

The buck was not the 10-point he was after. Instead, it was a dandy 8-pointer that McConnell couldn’t pass up.

McConnell drew his 64-inch, 55-pound longbow he’d made from an Osage orange tree limb. Then, shooting instinctively, he released a 26-inch arrow he’d made of cane and fletched with white turkey feathers.

His arrow hit the buck perfectly behind the shoulder, the super-sharp stone broadhead he handmade drove deep into the deer’s chest.

“I saw him race and tear through the corn field,” says McConnell, who owns a hardwood flooring and restoration business. “I heard him crashing away through corn stalks, and then nothing.”

The bowman found a good blood trail where the buck stood, and the trail was easy to follow through the corn.

“He only went about 75 yards before falling dead,” he says. “My stone broadhead did an incredible job, driving through its liver, a lung, and smashing into a rib on the opposite side of the buck. The arrow point hit bone and stopped or it would have completely gone through the buck’s hide on the opposite side.

“Everything really came together for me with that deer. I’ve taken lots of animals with the bows and arrows I’ve made. But it’s the first deer I’ve taken with a stone broadhead that I knapped myself – and it was devastating.”

McConnell has taken plenty of deer and wild hogs with traditional archery gear he’s made. He also shot a buffalo with his traditional equipment, and a stone point head he’d made specifically for the hunt.

He called some friends who helped get his big 8-point buck out of the field. The field-dressed buck weighed 180 pounds at the processor. It had an estimated live weight of well over 200 pounds.

He will eventually have the deer scored via Buckmasters, and his taxidermist estimated the rack to be about 140 inches.

Read Next: Killing a Grizzly the Old-Fashioned Way: With a Longbow and a Stone-Point Arrowhead

McConnell models his canted bow shooting style after legendary archer Howard Hill. He leans forward when drawing his bow, anchors his arrow at the corner of his mouth, and uses his bare hand holding his bow as an arrow rest.

“I got started in archery when I was only 14,” McConnell says. “It’s been a lot of fun learning about making bows, arrows, and now stone broadheads. Traditional equipment is remarkably efficient bowhunting gear.”

Bob McNally has been an outdoor writer since shortly after the earth’s crust cooled. He has written 12 outdoor books, more than 5,000 outdoor magazine stories (including many for Outdoor Life) and more newspaper outdoor columns and features than there are hairs on a grizzly bear.

Android Auto cutting off older devices, now requires Android 11 or newer

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Google has officially dropped support for some older Android versions in Android Auto, now requiring Android 11 or newer to function.

On a support page, Google says that Android 11.0 or newer is now required to use Android Auto:

An Android phone with Android 11.0 (Red Velvet Cake) or higher and an active data plan.

Previously, Google supported Android 9 and Android 10 for the in-car experience, a minimum requirement that itself was raised back in 2024 (but not enforced until 2025).

Some drivers started seeing a “Phone will no longer be supported” message in October 2026, but Google only updated its documentation this week. Android Auto 18.1, the latest app update, still supports Android 10.

Chicago Crowds Try and Fail to Move Anish Kapoor’s Bean, Art Lawyer Michael Ward Stout Has Died, Louvre President Says Mona Lisa’s Move Will Be Sad, and More

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

WHOSE BEAN CALLING? “Push the Bean!” shouted hundreds of people gathered Sunday in Chicago’s Millennium Park. They were there to answer a call to do just that: push Anish Kapoor’s iconic sculpture titled Cloud Gate (popularly referred to as the Bean) seven inches to the left of its location, reports CBS News. Legend and good-excuse-for-gathering-in-person has it that a man is stuck inside the bean and to free him, the sculpture could use a little nudge. The event has gone viral, with videos showing a crowd of outstretched hands reaching toward, trying and failing to push the 110-ton stainless steel sculpture, albeit mostly with just one hand, which arguably reduces their chance of success in half. Their other hands were busy recording with smartphones held high. After all, if a bean moves seven inches, but there is no one there to film and later post it, did it really move? Either way, concerned citizens should know that City Council member Brendan Reilly has long confirmed there is no man trapped inside the Bean. And also, please stop calling him about it. IN MEMORIAM. Michael Ward Stout, a lawyer who ran Robert Mapplethorpe’s foundation and represented Salvador Dalí, has died at 83, reports The New York Times. Stout handled artist licensing, contracts, exhibitions, and his other clients included the estates of Jean-Michel Basquiat and dealer Barbara Gladstone. We’ve also learned that French video artist Fred Forest died at 93, as reported by Le Monde. In 2017 and 2024, the Centre Pompidou dedicated retrospectives to his video, “sociological art,” and “Net art.” In a message sent by his son, the artist himself announced his own death. “Thanks to the affectionate complicity of my son Adrien, I have the pleasure of announcing my own death,” reads the statement. “Lying in this hole dug in the earth at Anserville [north of Paris], I will now have all the time to reflect calmly on what I will be doing tomorrow, my friends …” signed, Fred Forest.

Two oil studies by Peter Paul Rubens, lost for over 80 years, will join three others by the artist to be exhibited at the Ducal Museum in Gotha, Germany, where they had been part of the collection for centuries before their disappearance. [The Art Newspaper]

French investigators have arrested four people in connection with the July thefts of jewelry at the Musée Lalique, and ancient coins at the Archaeology Centre in Montans. [Le Monde]

Following news that The Hole gallery in New York is being sued over back rent and not paying artists and workers, the gallery is facing eviction from its Bowery space and is $102,823 in the hole for owed rent and late charges. [Artnet News]

More than 50 protesters gathered last week in Washington, D.C., to oppose President Trump’s planned triumphal arch project. [Hyperallergic]

The Grand Rapids Art Museum is staging a powerful survey of art by or about people once incarcerated within the United States, from a 1,000-piece ceramic installation by Julie Green of plates depicting the final-meal choices of people on death row, to quilted appliquéd works by artist Gary Tyler, who was wrongly convicted at seventeen years old. LONELY MONA LISA. Will the Mona Lisa’s smile feel lonelier if she’s removed from her current gallery in the Louvre and placed in a new one by herself? To Louvre president Christophe Leribault, at least, the prospect is decidedly triste, despite it being a key part of the museum’s major new, over $1-billion-dollar renovation plan for which he is actively fundraising. As he told the Wall Street Journal, Leribault explained that moving the Mona Lisa to her own room will control crowds, but “is sad,” because it “will separate the Mona Lisa from the other Leonardo da Vinci paintings.” For now, and despite planned protests, the Louvre remains open, and visitors can try to catch a glimpse of the Mona Lisa in the good company of other Da Vinci paintings and throngs of selfie-wielding tourists.

HBAR’s Path To $3 Relies On Volume, Not Promises

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Home » Altcoins » HBAR’s Path To $3 Relies On Volume, Not Promises

Analyst floats a $3 target for Hedera’s native digital currency as the DLT network snatches a fresh record.

Hedera Hashgraph’s (HBAR) price trades near $0.067 with a market capitalization around $3 billion and roughly 44.3 billion tokens in circulation out of a 50 billion supply.

A move to $3 would imply a valuation in the $133 – 150 billion range. That is aggressive, yet the case rests on capacity the network already demonstrates rather than planned upgrades.

Hedera has processed more than 20 million transactions in the recent month and recorded daily peaks above 800,000. Its governed throttle sits at 10,000 transactions per second for crypto transfers, with production bursts already surpassing that level.

🚨 #Hedera processed 21 MILLION transactions in 30 DAYS!!This is an ATH, $HBAR is averaging around 8 transactions EVERY SECOND, 24/7!This highlights the consistent activity on Hedera as its ecosystem expands across payments, tokenisation and enterprise applications… 🔥

The network does not require a consensus redesign to handle enterprise-scale settlement or micropayments. Most chains describe future capacity. Hedera is operating within measurable headroom today.

Several live deployments sit on the network. Verra is integrating the Guardian platform for carbon markets, while BCarbon has already moved millions of issued credits on-chain, shortening digital MRV timelines that previously stretched up to two years. Avery Dennison’s atma.io has tracked tens of billions of products on Hedera.

The UK’s first FX trades using tokenized RWAs as collateral.@Aberdeen_plc and @LloydsBank exchanged a tokenized money market fund and tokenized UK gilts, with @ArchaxEx issuing, transferring and holding the tokens. Powered by the Hedera network.Michael Donaldson, Aberdeen 🎥 pic.twitter.com/vSLsZnQ8vC

Lloyds and abrdn completed FX trades using tokenized money-market funds and gilts issued and managed on the network via Archax. Agent Accounts allow AI agents to execute bounded transactions with programmable limits, approved recipients, and audit trails. These are operating systems, not roadmap items.

HBAR remains roughly 88% below its 2021 all-time high near $0.57. Daily RSI has sat in the low-30s. In prior cycles, Layer-1 networks with measurable usage and institutional integrations saw large re-ratings once macro liquidity returned and the narrative shifted from pure speculation toward settlement infrastructure.

$3 hedera-hashgraph:native will happen — here’s how.Hedera HBAR is currently ~$0.067 with a ~$3B market cap and ~44.3B of 50B circulating. $3 implies a ~$133–150B valuation. That’s aggressive, but not unprecedented in a full risk-on cycle when a network already has real… pic.twitter.com/3mLw12oY7R

From there, continued volume growth on the live use cases above would be required to treat $1 as an intermediate level and $3 as a cycle high rather than a stretch target.

The pieces for higher sustained volume already exist: digital carbon registries tied to major standards bodies, production supply-chain tracking at billions of units, institutional collateral workflows, and AI-agent payment primitives.

When the next liquidity wave arrives, networks that can absorb real volume without congestion or unpredictable fees are the ones positioned to re-rate. Hedera is one of the few that already shows that capacity in production.

The path from current HBAR price levels to $3 is volume plus narrative plus cycle conditions. It is not a single announcement. The infrastructure is running. The rest remains speculative.

Mancini explains Calò omission from Italy squad and hits back at player’s agent

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Roberto Mancini has explained why he decided to leave Frosinone midfielder Giacomo Calò  out of his latest Italy national team squad, and has hit back at the player’s agent, who had accused the CT of not showing logic in his selections.

Newly-promoted Frosinone have been one of the surprise packages of the 2026-27 Serie A season, currently sitting above Juventus in sixth place, and Calò, who has five goals and an assist from 11 league appearances in central midfield so far, has been an instrumental figure behind their success.

Calò‘s agent, Vincenzo Pisacane, was keen to point out that his client is Italy’s leading goalscorer from central midfield in Serie A this season, and said that he hopes this ‘isn’t the death of meritocracy’ when it comes to national team call-ups.

“The players Mancini called up are all very strong, but I don’t think any of them have Calò’s numbers at the moment,” said Calò’s agent in a recent interview with TMW.

“That’s why it would have been right to call him up. I hope yesterday’s omission isn’t the death of meritocracy and that Giacomo can be called up later if he keeps this up. Otherwise, it means there’s no logic to the national team call-ups.”

Mancini has since had an opportunity to respond at his press conference at Coverciano on Monday.

He said that there is still a possibility that Calò is called up in the future, but explained that he wanted to take a look at a number of younger profiles in this camp. He also made it clear that he will not be dictated to by players’ agents.

“Now we need to find different solutions and try to make young players earn experience,” Mancini explained on Monday.

“Calò is one of the players doing well. Frosinone have 3-4 players who are good. We can’t call players who are already 29 or 30 right now. When we need them, we’ll do it, but not because his agent tells us so.”

That’s a Mora: Rodrigo and Roma rise with Lazio to give Eternal City new life

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Roma and Lazio have both made unbeaten starts to the Serie A season, giving those in the Italian capital dreams of a Scudetto

After nine games in 28 days, Gian Piero Gasperini finally got a moment to pause and reflect on Sunday night. The Roma manager was back in Liguria, where he worked for the best part of a decade across two stints at Genoa, to collect an award from Stelle Nello Sport – an association formed to promote sport in the region – celebrating his career achievements and commitment to courageous attacking football.

The timing worked out well. Gasperini’s Roma thumped Sassuolo 3-0 on Saturday, bouncing back from a midweek loss to Manchester United. That result meant the Giallorossi had scored 31 times in 11 Serie A games. In the last 65 years only two Italian teams – the Juventus and Napoli sides of 2017-18 – had ever made a more prolific start to a top-flight campaign. More importantly, Roma’s victory meant they remained undefeated in domestic competition this season. Just like their neighbours Lazio, who drew 1-1 away to Napoli in Sunday’s early kick-off.

Roma Caput Mundi? The old Latin phrase positions Rome as the capital of the world. Probably a little bit of a stretch, given Roma’s result against United. Lazio are not even competing in European competition this season and their own manager, Gennaro Gattuso, keeps claiming his priority is to avoid relegation.

Still, this is an extraordinary moment for football in the capital. According to Opta, this is the first time two teams from the same city have ever made it through 11 games of a Serie A season without a single defeat. When you consider that Rome’s two leading clubs have won just five Scudetti between them – as compared to 40 combined for Internazionale and Milan – the statistic jumps out even more.

Acknowledging this detail in an interview after collecting his award, Gasperini could not help but draw a contrast between his team and their rivals. “It’s a bit of a surprise for everyone to have this Scudetto derby situation playing out in November,” he said. “But it’s a reflection of the values of the city. “Rome is an extraordinary city which has a huge enthusiasm for football. For Roma the stadium is always full. Lazio might have some other problems. But the passion for football in Rome is incredible.”

His reference was to the mass boycott of Lazio’s home games by supporters, the reasons for which have been covered in recent editions of this column. Attendance figures for the Biancocelesti’s home games at the Stadio Olimpico this season hover at about 10,000 people. Roma drew 64,674 for Sassuolo on Saturday – in line with their average. Ticket sales do not decide football games. Lazio sit top of the table heading into the international break. But Roma have plenty to feel good about too.

Sassuolo were about the best team they could have faced this weekend. Seeking a bounce-back after their collapse in Manchester, Roma lined up against opponents who had earned a single point from their previous six games and owned the leakiest defence in the division.

Leonardo Balerdi had a header cleared off the line before Mario Hermoso put Roma ahead in the 27th minute, punishing some indecisive defending. Moments later, a scuffed shot from Donyell Malen – bobbling far off target – took deviations off a pair of Sassuolo players and wound up in the back of the net. The only question left was how many goals Roma would add. In the end it was just one, Rodrigo Mora cutting in from the left and swapping passes with Malen before striking back across his body into the bottom corner from the right side of the box.

A third consecutive Serie A game with a goal for the 19-year-old, who also set up Matías Soulé’s opener against United. And the Portuguese even played the initial ball to create the chance that led to their penalty in that game as well.

Gasperini has never been shy in his enthusiasm for Mora. “We’re standing before an extraordinary talent,” said the manager after including the teenager in his starting XI for a season-opening 4-0 win over Fiorentina in August. “Every time he touches the ball something beautiful happens.”

is not alone in noticing. Mora was called up by Portugal this week for their Nations League games against Denmark and Wales. He has trained with the senior team before but not played for them. There will be greater opportunities to do so now Cristiano Ronaldo has retired from international football, though Mora expressed regret, too, at not getting to train again with a man he described as “my idol”.

Back at Roma, Gasperini has tried not to ask too much, too soon, of Mora, giving him a full 90 minutes only once, in a Champions League rout of Slovan Bratislava. Otherwise, he has alternated starts with opportunities from the bench during this busy early-season stretch.

Only in the last week did that change. Mora started against Udinese, scoring the second goal in a 5-2 win, then was back in the lineup to face United. Gasperini’s decision to take him off at half-time of that game has been widely criticised. These things are always easy to oversimplify, but it is true that Roma’s attacking threat seemed to vanish with Mora missing after the interval.

Venezia 2-1 Udinese, Torino 1-1 Lecce, Cagliari 1-0 Frosinone, Parma 1-2 Bologna, Roma 3-0 Sassuolo, Napoli 1-1 Lazio, Monza 0-1 Atalanta, Genoa 1-2 Milan, Inter 2-0 Como, Fiorentina 2-2 Juventus

Gasperini spoke at the end of that defeat of it being a lesson that Roma could learn from. His team is still a work in progress, but the trend lines are more than encouraging. Roma secured their Champions League spot by winning six of their final seven games last season. They have not lost a domestic home game for more than 11 months and even their most recent away defeat in Serie A, to Inter, was all the way back in April.

Mora’s rapid ascent has added a new wrinkle. The attack we have seen in Roma’s last three games, with him and Soulé playing either side of Malen in a 3-4-2-1 has carved opponents open with such devastating effectiveness that you could start to wonder whether a healthy Paulo Dybala – for so long the standout talent here – would displace any of them from a first-choice XI.

This is not really a debate anyone needs to have. Roma hope to get Dybala back from his calf injury after the international round, but his body has always imposed breaks for him in every season and the Giallorossi will need rotation in any case to keep everyone fresh through a long campaign.

The improved depth of Roma’s squad, on the other hand, is a reason to believe that they can sustain their strong start. Gasperini, like most managers, would prefer to talk about the next game than discuss title ambitions this early in a campaign, but when a reporter asked if he would sign up now for a guaranteed second place he dismissed the idea out of hand.

“We don’t need to give ourselves false illusions, but we’re not going to knock ourselves down for no reason either,” he said. “I don’t know what we can achieve.”

Scrambled Eggs Are 10x Creamier & Fluffier With One Boxed Ingredient

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

When you make scrambled eggs, heat, fat content, and your tools often require you to make them either fluffy or creamy, not both. However, powdered milk is an interesting ingredient that can help you bridge the divide, imparting richness and enabling volume, provided you use it correctly.

Powdered milk can be made from skim milk, whole milk, and buttermilk, but all are essentially just milk with the water taken out and converted into a soluble powder. When it comes to water versus milk in scrambled eggs, folks typically favor the latter because it gives a bit of extra richness and flavor. However, it can weigh them down, unlike water, which steams to fluff up the little curds into pillowy softness. While you can't just add water to powdered milk eggs because you'd just be using milk with extra steps, it does allow you to employ other tricks that increase the fluffiness, giving you the best of both worlds.

Powdered milk is also quite a bit more convenient since it's shelf-stable and dry. Not only does it last longer than regular milk, but you can also toast the powder to lend a nutty savoriness similar to browned butter. Roughly a tablespoon per three eggs is all you need to achieve serious flavor. Nonfat varieties typically last much longer than full-fat varieties, which tend to have a shorter shelf life. Freezing it can extend its shelf life even further, so it's an easy and cheap ingredient to keep on hand.

The biggest benefit of powdered versus liquid milk is that it adds milk solids without adding extra water. This helps you avoid making your scrambled eggs too watery and is why, while it may not make them fluffy on its own, it also won't inhibit other tricks. There's no shortage of expert tips for restaurant-quality eggs, but try focusing on ones that add a bit of extra air and/or cook them quickly for the best results.

You'll want to thoroughly incorporate your powdered milk, but luckily, a thorough whisking also helps introduce some air into the eggs. The goal is to make your mixture paler and more voluminous, looking as if you added extra liquid. The mason jar hack of shaking your eggs is not only easier than learning a fancy technique, but also helps ensure your powder is dispersed properly. If you're making a ton, you can even repurpose a protein shaker bottle and let that little metal whisk ball do the heavy lifting for you.

Slowly bringing your eggs to temperature is a French style that gives them a creamy, porridge-like consistency. However, this also slowly coagulates the proteins and prevents them from fluffing up properly. The key is to cook them fast enough that their natural moisture steams, but not so hot that they turn rubbery. Full-fat milk powder can help contribute to a tender texture and, when used in conjunction with moderate heat, help produce eggs that are moist but fluffy, creamy but firm.

Next Crystal Palace manager: Contenders to replace Pierre Sage as clear favourite emerges

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Pierre Sage will be sacked as Crystal Palace manager following Sunday’s 3-1 home defeat by Liverpool.

The Frenchman’s job has been under serious scrutiny for weeks now, with Palace heading into the November international break sitting rock bottom of the Premier League table after winning just two and losing seven of their first 10 games this season.

Sage has struggled to get his ideas across since taking over from Oliver Glasner in the summer, having enjoyed a terrific campaign with Lens last term.

The Eagles have the worst defence in the Premier League with 24 goals conceded so far, and Sage has run out of time to convince Palace’s decision-makers that he is the right man for the job.

With an announcement on Sage’s future imminent, they have already sounded out potential replacements.

Standard Sport has assessed who could take over at Selhurst Park next...

Out of work since being sacked by Tottenham in February, Thomas Frank was one of several candidates that Palace looked at to succeed Glasner in the summer and is the early frontrunner to replace Sage.

Crystal Palace set to sack Sage with club bottom of Premier League

Frank tipped to be next Crystal Palace manager as new rival emerges

When is FA Cup draw? Start time, ball numbers and TV channel today

Frank’s reputation is in need of rehabilitation, and it is unclear how much of a toll his torrid eight-month spell in charge of Spurs took, but he remains highly regarded after his work with Brentford.

Frank released a statement in June saying he was taking some time out of football before his next job, so the Dane may be unwilling to jump into a role so soon after the season has started.

Another manager who stepped away from football over the summer, Kieran McKenna made the shock decision to leave Ipswich after earning promotion back to the Premier League.

One of Britain’s most highly sought-after young coaches, McKenna’s decision to spend more time with his family came as a surprise, but potentially hints at the fact that he is not yet ready to get back into management.

McKenna’s patient, possession-based style would constitute a dramatic change in tactics for Palace, but the Northern Irishman is comfortable using a three-man defence when his team has the ball.

Bringing in former Newcastle boss Eddie Howe would be considered quite the coup for Palace.

The Englishman has attracted interest from Manchester United amid ongoing doubts over Michael Carrick’s future and guided Newcastle to Carabao Cup glory in 2025.

Howe is actively open to new opportunities, but is said to be willing to wait for an elite, high-profile job, which could leave Palace out of the running.

Arguably the most divisive name on the list, Sean Dyche’s pragmatic approach is a far cry from the expansive, counter-attacking football Palace fans have been used to under Glasner and, to a lesser extent, Sage.

Dyche’s Premier League experience, especially given that Palace will have one eye on ensuring they stay in the top-flight, makes him an attractive proposition for those in the boardroom.

Whether they would be prepared to face the inevitable backlash his appointment would prompt is a different question entirely, though.

Coventry boss Frank Lampard is reportedly under consideration for the Palace job despite signing only a new contract with the promoted Sky Blues in June.

Lampard is a long-standing target and one of several other candidates Palace are reportedly eyeing up alongside Kim Hellberg and Vitor Matos, who have led Middlesbrough and Swansea, respectively, towards the top of the Championship table this term.

Free agent Vitor Pereira is also said to be on Palace’s shortlist, while Australia head coach and former Palace captain Tony Popovic could be a surprise contender.

England assistant Anthony Barry is a risky but potentially intriguing appointment, while former Wolves boss Rob Edwards is well known to Palace sporting director Matt Hobbs.

Dashboard Confessional Announce 2027 Unplugged: 25th Anniversary Tour

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Dashboard Confessional will celebrate the 25th anniversary of their seminal Unplugged performance with a 15-date North American tour. The trek kicks off in Chicago on March 12 and continues through April 3, when it wraps in San Francisco. It will hit cities like New York, Boston, Nashville, and Los Angeles.

“Dashboard Confessional’s Unplugged was a pivotal and unifying moment for the band and our fans,” frontman Chris Carrabba said in a statement. “I’m so excited to celebrate that moment in time. I’ve recently done some thrillingly small solo acoustic shows in Nashville, New York and Los Angeles — the energy around those shows was, fittingly, electric. I’m excited to carry that spirit into bigger venues with the band arrangements from our original Unplugged performance.”

Dashboard Confessional performed on MTV’s Unplugged series on April 24, 2002. It was released as an album, MTV Unplugged 2.0, that December, a year ahead of their 2003 breakout LP, A Mark, a Mission, a Brand, a Scar.

Ticket information for the tour is available on the band’s website. The Saints & Sailors Fan Club presale begins Nov. 12 at 9 a.m. local. Prediction: Ivory Coast vs Gambia

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Ivory Coast and Gambia will meet at Stade de la Paix in Bouake on Thursday, November 12, as the two early leaders in Group C continue their qualification campaigns for the 2027 Africa Cup of Nations.

Both teams have collected six points from their opening two matches. Ivory Coast has done so without conceding, while Gambia arrives after scoring six goals against Somalia and Ghana.

Verdict: Over 2.5 goals Best odds: 16/25 Bookmaker: 1xBet

Ivory Coast has made a convincing start to qualifying, beating Ghana and Somalia by the same 2-0 scoreline. Those results have put the Elephants in a strong position before a pair of matches against their closest challenger.

Gambia has already won away to Ghana and has enough attacking quality to cause problems if the home side commits too many players forward.

Still, Ivory Coast has several ways to stretch the visiting defense. Amad Diallo, Nicolas Pepe and Yan Diomande give Renard options out wide, where beating the first defender could force Gambia’s central players out of position.

That should be an important part of the home side’s approach. Moving the ball quickly between the wings would make it harder for Gambia to protect the middle and give Ivory Coast opportunities to deliver into the penalty area.

The Elephants won the most recent meeting 1-0 in March 2025. Their opening qualifying results also suggest they can manage a game without allowing much at the other end.

However, Gambia’s attacking form gives this meeting a different feel. Ivory Coast may need to keep looking for goals even after taking the lead.

Renard has confirmed that Amad Diallo is fit following the injury concern that forced him off during his club’s match on October 31.

Odilon Kossounou and Guela Doue are absent from the squad. Evan Ndicka, Ousmane Diomande and Emmanuel Agbadou remain available in central defense, while Oumar Solet is among the new additions to the group.

Franck Kessie and Ibrahim Sangare offer experience in midfield, with Malick Yalcouye another option.

Further forward, Renard can choose from Amad, Pepe, Yan Diomande, Bazoumana Toure and Martial Godo, alongside central attacking options Elye Wahi and Bonny.

There is enough variety to change the attack during the match, particularly if Gambia manages to close the spaces around its penalty area.

Gambia’s opening results deserve attention. The Scorpions began with a 2-1 victory over Somalia before producing a 4-2 comeback win away to Ghana.

That second performance showed how dangerous this team can be when a match opens up. Gambia trailed 2-0 in Accra before Mohamed Badamosi and Omar Colley brought it level before halftime. Ebrima Colley then scored twice after the break to complete the turnaround.

Recovering from that position should give Johnathan McKinstry’s players confidence heading into another difficult away assignment. They have already shown that falling behind does not have to end their chances.

The defensive side of those performances is worth considering, though. Gambia conceded against Somalia and allowed Ghana to establish a two-goal lead. Similar lapses in Bouake could prove expensive.

McKinstry will probably want his midfield to stay close to the defense while leaving enough support for counterattacks. Sitting too deep for long periods would invite repeated pressure from Ivory Coast’s wide players.

There should be opportunities to break when the hosts push forward. Whether Gambia can use those moments effectively may determine how competitive this match becomes.

Gambia has been boosted by the return of Musa Barrow and Yankuba Minteh, who missed the opening two qualifiers through injury.

Their availability gives McKinstry additional attacking options alongside Ebrima Colley and Badamosi, both of whom contributed to the successful start.

However, Jesper Ceesay and Suleiman Camara have subsequently withdrawn because of injuries. Saidou Khan and Salieu Drammeh have been called up as their replacements.

Alieu Fadera is also unavailable with a thigh injury. Omar Colley offers experience in defense, while Ebou Adams and Abubakarr Barry are among the midfield options.

The returning attackers should help Gambia carry a threat beyond its own half. McKinstry will need that outlet if his team spends long stretches defending.

Ivory Coast should create enough chances to put Gambia’s defense under sustained pressure. The visitors have conceded in both qualifiers, and recovering from another poor start would be difficult against this opposition.

Gambia also has reasons to believe it can contribute to the scoring. Six goals in two qualifiers, followed by the return of Barrow and Minteh, give the away side a credible attacking threat.

Another controlled 2-0 home win is the main concern for this selection. Even so, the attacking options available and Gambia’s defensive vulnerabilities make over 2.5 goals the preferred bet.

Verdict: Over 2.5 goals Best odds: 16/25 Bookmaker: 1xBet

Les U16 n'ont pas fait de détail

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Après leur déconvenue d'il y a trois semaines, et la défaite face au CASCO Oullins à l'extrême limite du temps additionnel (3-2), les U16 de Kevin De Jesus avaient à coeur de réagir. La trêve avait été meublée par une rencontre amicale à Bourg-Peronnas remportée par 3 à 0 (buts de N.Fontaine et Joris Rochette 2), histoire de garder le rythme et de parfaire les automatismes. Les Verts se présentaient hier, sur le terrain synthétique Georges Bereta, avec une équipe dans laquelle figuraient quatre U15 (Ilan Clapeyron, Riad Moutae, Kaïs Boukerma et Hugo Durin) face à la lanterne rouge, l'AFCA (Aurillac Foot Cantal Auvergne) qui, il faut bien l'avouer, n'a pas opposé une farouche résistance aux Verts. Ces derniers l'ont en effet emporté sur le large score de 9 à 0, alors qi'ils menaient déjà 4-0 au repos.

Ce match a ressemblé à une attaque-défense, digne d'un match de handball. Dès la 2ème minute, Hugo Durin se mettait déjà en évidence avec un tir au ras du poteau. Dans la foulée, à la réception d'un ballon venu de la droite, il éliminait son adversaire et marquait  d'un tir au ras du sol. (ASSE-AFCA : 1-0, 3ème).Le jeu se déroulait devant le but aurillacois et Rayan Fontaine, servi par Adem Moussaoui, d'un tir dans un angle fermé aggravait le score. (ASSE-AFCA : 2-0, 7ème)La domination verte ne se relâchait pas : sur un corner, la reprise de la tête de Raphaël Moser était détournée par le gardien (17ème), un coup-franc de Lenny Nebout, tiré à ras de terre, était repoussé par le portier cantalou et la reprise de Noah Fontaine ne trouvait pas le cadre (18ème), un tir de loin de Dixon Ahamadi était une nouvelle fois détourné (22ème). Entre temps, une première incursion aurillacoise était ponctuée par un corner, mais la reprise de la tête n,'inquiétait pas Ilan Clapeyron.  Les Verts allaient de nouveau trouver la faille : à la suite d'une combinaison avec Noah Fontaine, Hugo Durin était trouvé dans un petit espace et sa frappe croisée du gauche faisait mouche. (ASSE-AFCA : 3-0, 24ème).Dès la remise en jeu, le défenseur central Idris Balta s'avançait, plaçait un tir qui rebondissait devant le but et trompait le gardien adverse. (ASSE-AFCA : 4-0, 25ème).A la 30ème minute, suite à un centre de Raphaël Moser, Hugo Durin remisait sur Joris Rochette (photo), mais la reprise de ce dernier était contrée. Aurillac réagissait timidement à la 38ème et sur un tir venu de loin, Ilan Clapeyron se détendait bien. C'était un petit intermède au milieu de la domination stéphanoise qui ne se démentait pas : un coup-franc de Rayan Fontaine trouvait la tête de Dixon Ahamadi, mais le gardien adverse captait le cuir (40ème), une frappe lointaine de Joris Rochette s'écrasait sur l'équerre du but cantalou (43ème), sur une combinaison côté gauche, le tir de Raphaël Moser dans un angle fermé frôlait la transversale (44ème), et, enfin à la 45ème, un tir dévié de Joris Rochette, manquait de tromper le gardien. La mi-temps était sifflée sur ces entrefaites. Le score de 4 à 0 illustrait la mainmise stéphanoise.

Le second acte repartait avec la même physionomie. A la 50ème une subtile combinaison Joris Rochette - Hugo Durin butait sur le gardien. A la 52ème, Joris Rochette repiquait au centre et sa frappe du gauche passait à côté. La minute suivante Hugo Durin alertait Rayan Fontaine et le centre de ce dernier trouvait la tête de son frère Noah, mais sa reprise était détournée en corner, corner sur lequel la tête de Dixon Ahamadi ne trouvait pas le cadre. A la 55ème, Rayan Fontaine était fauché à l'entrée de la surface et le coup-franc de Raphaël Moser s'écrasait sur la transversale aurillacoise. A force d'appuyer, les Verts allaient trouver la faille : une frappe de 25 mètres de Dixon Ahamadi était détournée par le gardien et sur le corner qui suivait Rayan Fontaine trouvait la tête d'Hugo Durin. (ASSE-AFCA : 5-0, 56ème) A la 62ème, Hugo Durin servait latéralement Lenny Nebout mais la frappe de ce dernier était stoppée par le gardien. Servi par une passe verticale d'Idris Balta, Kaïs Boukerma voyait son tir détourné par le portier aurillacois (65ème). A la 69ème, Rayan Fontaine trouvait Hugo Durin dans le dos de la défense et ce dernier concluait du droit. (ASSE-AFCA : 6-0, 69ème).Peu après, Hugo Durin, alerté par Joris Rochette, partait de la ligne médiane, prenait de vitesse la défense adverse et s'en allait marquer du droit. (ASSE-AFCA : 7-0, 72ème).Joris Rochette faisait un festival côté droit et son centre en retrait trouvait Riad Moutae qui  parachevait cette belle action. (ASSE-AFCA : 8-0, 76ème).Enfin, alors qu'il restait une dizaine de minutes à jouer, une belle passe cachée de Lenny Nebout pour Hugo Durin permettait à ce dernier de conclure d'un tir entre le gardien et son poteau d'une frappe décroisée. C'était son 6ème but de l'après-midi ! (ASSE-AFCA : 9-0, 81ème). Le match à sens unique se terminait sur ce score fleuve qui reflétait bien la sérieuse prestation des jeunes Verts.

La composition : Clapeyron - R.Fontaine, Ahamadi, Balta, Moser - Evaristo, Rochette, Moussaoui (Boukerma, 60ème)- Nebout, Durin, N.Fontaine (Moutae, 60ème).

La réaction de Kevin de Jesus : "Satisfait du match ? Certes, mais mon but c'est de voir des comportements qui progressent, qui avancent vers ce que j'exige des jeunes. A la mi-temps, le résultat semblait acquis, mais j'ai demandé à mes joueurs de continuer à trouver des solutions pour déséquilibrer l'équipe adverse, de varier les combinaisons pour arriver à cet objectif, en diversifiant les circuits, en impliquant toute l'équipe dans cette recherche, défense y compris, par les montées des excentrés dans leur couloir, des centraux dans l'axe pour tenter des frappes de loin. J'ai apprécié le sérieux des joueurs qui sont restés sérieux, sans s'enflammer, en respectant l'adversaire. Cet esprit-là est important."
