"Mario Is My Life" - Beloved Blogger 'Supper Mario Broth' Reveals Health Issues

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

"Only blindness or death will result in me no longer posting Mario facts"

The author of the beloved Mario-related blog Supper Mario Broth has posted a health update revealing he has heart failure.

This comes via a social post in which he states he has "a 2/3 chance of surviving past one year with this condition." Despite "a lifelong commitment to a healthy lifestyle", he says his decline in health is due to underlying genetic causes.

A complication from his diagnosis is retinopathy, which is essentially the degradation of the retina that can result in a loss of sight and may affect his ability to research Mario games, post facts, and run the blog in the future.

I am scared I will soon not be able to see Mario again. However, I promise that until that day arrives, I will always be here, posting Mario facts. Thank you all so much for your support through the years.

Despite this news, he has stressed an ongoing commitment to Supper Mario Broth, stating that "only blindness or death will result in me no longer posting Mario facts." He goes on to say that the announcement is not a request for help before thanking readers for all their support over the years.

His posts have been inundated with support. User 'rainbow batsuit' says, "Even if you never post a single fact again, the legacy of joy and discovery you have already built will live on in our hearts for untold years." All of us here at Nintendo Life wholeheartedly agree.

The blog's latest post relating to Mario reveals that it's actually possible to use Dry Bones in Super Mario Maker 2 to play the ocarina in Zelda: Ocarina of Time on Switch 2 via the console's built-in mic.

Military radar can make your router switch Wi-Fi channels - here's why

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

You might notice that every so often, your dual-band router drops its 5 GHz network without warning. Phones and laptops connected to it drop down to the slower 2.4 GHz band, video calls begin to stutter and freeze, and a few minutes later, everything comes back on a different channel. If you take a look in your router's event log afterward, you might notice the word "RADAR" appearing throughout.

That's Dynamic Frequency Selection, or DFS, in action. According to ASUS, it's how the router gives way to military radar, satellite communication and weather radar that has priority on large parts of the 5 GHz band. In the United States, Federal Communications Commission (FCC) rules require a router to listen for radar on one of those shared channels for 60 seconds before it transmits, to stop within 10 seconds of hearing one and to stay off that channel for at least 30 minutes afterward. DFS is switched on by default, and the FCC requires it to stay that way in the shared bands.

The radar that's most likely to interfere with a US home network isn't the military, though, it's those found at airports. The Federal Aviation Administration (FAA) runs 45 Terminal Doppler Weather Radar systems that protect 46 of the country's busiest airports, from JFK and O'Hare to Dallas and Denver. Their job is to spot wind shear, the sudden and potentially dangerous change in wind speed and direction that has historically caused aviation accidents during approach and departure; they transmit between 5600 and 5650 MHz right in the middle of the 5 GHz Wi-Fi band. Per the FAA, no wind shear accident has occurred at a TDWR-protected airport since the system was commissioned in 1994.

In early 2009, the FAA began detecting interference to those radars caused by unlicensed 5 GHz equipment, prompting a joint investigation with the National Telecommunications and Information Administration (NTIA). Enforcement followed, and in August 2019, the FCC proposed $25,000 fines against three wireless ISPs — Boom Solutions, Integra Wireless and WinPR — after their equipment interfered with the weather radar at the San Juan International Airport in Puerto Rico. In each case, the FCC said the companies had turned DFS off. WinPR's penalty was confirmed that November and Boom Solutions' in early 2020. While those were outdoor point-to-point links and not home routers, they're a good example of why router manufacturers can't simply drop a feature because it annoys their customers.

That government-mandated 60-second listen happens each time a router spins up on a DFS channel, which is why 5 GHz can take longer than 2.4 GHz to reappear after you restart your router. ASUS says the wait can take anywhere from one to 10 minutes depending on the country. The upper end of the scale is in Europe, where the ETSI standard governing 5 GHz Wi-Fi demands a 10-minute check on any channel that overlaps the 5600 to 5650 MHz weather radar slot.

Radar detected mid-session triggers the 10-second exit and 30-minute lockout. FRITZ! says its routers steer connected devices to 2.4 GHz while they wait, "seamlessly" for devices that support the 802.11k and 802.11v roaming standards and by briefly dropping the connection for older ones. ASUS, meanwhile, notes that a radar hit on a 160 MHz channel can push the router down to 80 MHz, and that a router set to a fixed channel won't move back once the radar goes away.

So, it's fine to use DFS channels. They're legal, enabled out of the box and most of the 5 GHz band is made up of them; only channels 36 to 48 and 149 to 165 are radar-free in the United States. The trade-off is occasional interruptions if you live near somewhere like an airport or military base. FRITZ!'s threshold is radar detected continuously or every hour, at which point it recommends pinning the router to channels 36 to 48.

ASUS provides instructions for disabling automatic DFS channels that also tell users to untick 160 MHz channel bandwidth, because the widest 5 GHz channels can't be built without the radar-shared spectrum.

So, by turning DFS off, you'll get some extra stability at the cost of losing out on the fastest 5 GHz connection your router can offer. That's worth thinking about before you go hunting for tweaks that could speed up your router. You'll find these settings alongside the options you'd change to improve your router's security, usually under the 5 GHz wireless menu.

Blackburn Rovers, Birmingham City track Chelsea starlet after Tony Mowbray snubbed summer loan deal

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Blackburn Rovers and Birmingham City have both performed positively over the first quarter of the 2026/27 campaign, but they’ll have eyes on the January transfer window as an opportunity to strengthen the ranks further. Now, TEAMtalk has claimed one player of interest to both clubs is Kendry Paez.

The report states that Chelsea are actively looking for potential loan destinations for Ecuadorian prospect Paez. Rovers and the Blues are keen, but the 19-year-old also has admirers at Celtic and Trabzonspor, and he was eyed by Belgium’s KV Mechelen in the summer.

Paez was on the radar at Ewood Park in the last transfer window as was put to Tony Mowbray as a potential signing. However, the boss snubbed the deal, and the attacking midfielder has remained with Chelsea instead.

Paez was viewed as one of South America’s most exciting talents when he officially joined Chelsea in 2025. However, loans at RC Strasbourg and River Plate have not yielded the success hoped.

Blackburn Rovers were lined up as a potential destination in Paez’s bid to settle into English football, but after Mowbray opted against bringing him in, he has only played U21s football.

Paez has made six appearances across the Premier League 2 and EFL Trophy. He provided an assist in a 2-1 defeat to Wycombe Wanderers in September and played a little over an hour in a 2-0 triumph over Reading in the same cup competitions in October.

At 19, the Chelsea starlet is still young and has plenty of time to come good in England. But, Paez has struggled to impact games the way he did in his emergence in Ecuador with Independiente del Valle, so it remains to be seen if a half-season loan to the Championship can breathe life into his career again.

This applies to Birmingham City more given that they are the current Championship leaders, but clubs have to be careful with loan deals in the winter. Recruitment staff and managers alike need to be wary of how new signings will impact a thriving squad.

Loan players come in with a desire to play, but it can be tough to find opportunities in a thriving team. If that were to happen to Paez, it wouldn’t help his development and could bring players off the pitch too.

Blackburn Rovers aren’t in quite the same position as they’re 13th, but Mowbray has harnessed some strong squad spirit. The fact the boss has already snubbed the loan signing of Paez would raise questions over a winter deal for his services too.

Time will tell what comes of Paez’s winter transfer fate as Chelsea look for potential destinations.

Apple Intelligence registered in China for use on iPads and Macs

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Shanghai’s cyberspace regulator approved the filing weeks before Apple’s AI service, powered by Alibaba’s Qwen, launches in China.

The Apple Store in Nanjing, China, lit up at night.

Apple’s AI service, Apple Intelligence, has been cleared for use on tablets and laptops in China. The cyberspace regulator in Shanghai made the change on Monday, Reuters reported.

The regulator said Apple’s iPads and Mac laptops had been added as two new uses in the service’s filing. It made the change under China’s rules for AI services.

In China, firms must register AI models and chatbot-style services with the state before they can offer them to the public.

Apple Intelligence first won approval for use on iPhones in China in mid-July. At the time, Alibaba said its Qwen AI model would power the service on Apple’s devices in China. Baidu is also a tech partner, Bloomberg reported.

“Qwen will be integrated into Apple Intelligence experiences within iOS, iPadOS, macOS, and visionOS for users in China,” an Alibaba spokesperson told CNBC in July.

Apple first showed Apple Intelligence in 2024 and began rolling it out in other markets later that year, with iOS 18. China has waited much longer.

Apple needs local partners in China. OpenAI’s ChatGPT, which runs some Apple Intelligence tools in other countries, is not offered there.

On 31 October, Apple said on its China news site that the first features would come with iOS 27.3. A test version for app makers went live on 4 November. The full release is due in December.

In China, Qwen will run Siri, Writing Tools, and visual intelligence, which lets users ask about what the camera sees. But Siri AI and other tools Apple showed off at its June developer event are not part of the launch.

Apple’s October news listed only iPhones. These are the iPhone 15 Pro and 15 Pro Max, the iPhone Air, the iPhone Duo, and every model from the iPhone 16 on. Siri and the phone must be set to English or Simplified Chinese.

Reuters also reported in August that Apple had built its own AI model just for China with Alibaba’s help. That was a shift from its plan to rely only on outside models in the country.

Apple did not say on Monday when the features will reach iPads and Macs in China.

Researchers warn that warmer weather is helping mosquitoes spread dangerous diseases like malaria and dengue

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

A century of data shows bed nets, drugs and growing cities explain much of why malaria and dengue went separate ways as the planet warmed.

Scientists have long predicted that a warmer world would bring more disease from mosquitoes, and dengue has kept rising along with the heat. Malaria went the other way.

Over a century of increasing temperatures, the area where malaria was a constant presence shrank to about half of what it had been, while dengue infections climbed to record highs.

According to a new review of the evidence, much of that split comes from outside the weather. People pushed malaria back with decades of bed nets, spraying, and drugs, while the growth of cities favored the mosquitoes that carry dengue.

Toby Cumming, a research fellow at the Health and Climate Initiative in Monash University’s School of Public Health, led the work with three colleagues. It is a review of studies that other teams have already published, not a new experiment.

In an interview with Earth.com, Cumming said the climate in many parts of the world is becoming more suitable for diseases spread by mosquitoes. “We cannot draw a straight line from climate suitability to rates of malaria and dengue transmission,” Cumming added.

Both diseases reach people through a bite. Malaria comes from a parasite carried by Anopheles mosquitoes, which bite mostly at night. Dengue comes from a virus carried by Aedes mosquitoes, which bite during the day.

Mosquitoes are cold-blooded, so their body temperature follows the air around them. At 59°F (15°C), a young Aedes aegypti takes 40 days to become an adult. At 95°F (35°C), it takes seven.

Warmth also makes mosquitoes bite more often. It also shortens the time the parasite or virus needs to develop inside the insect before it can infect a person.

There’s a ceiling, though. Past a certain point, heat starts to hurt the mosquito. Models suggest malaria spreads best at about 77°F to 82°F (25°C to 28°C). For dengue, it’s about 79°F to 84°F (26°C to 29°C).

Rain works in both directions. Heavy downpours can wash eggs and larvae away, while drought can leave stagnant pools in riverbeds and push families to store water in open containers near their homes.

Asked how people can protect themselves from dengue, Cumming said the best thing is to avoid bites, with long, loose clothing and insect repellent. Emptying containers that hold water around the home helps too.

The authors put numbers on how much warming has already shifted the odds. Between the 1950s and the decade ending in 2024, the climate’s estimated potential to spread dengue rose 12 percent for Aedes aegypti and 49 percent for its cousin, Aedes albopictus.

Over the same stretch, the area where the climate suits malaria grew by only about 2 percent. In Africa, the malaria map is moving.

One analysis cited in the review found that the elevation limit of malaria mosquitoes rose about 21 feet (6.5 meters) a year from 1898 to 2016.

Even so, the authors call the evidence for climate-driven shifts in malaria more contested than for dengue.

Dengue’s spread is easier to see. The disease has spread locally in places where it hadn’t before, including the southern United States, and it’s showing up more often in mainland France. Earth.com has also reported on rising dengue risk in California.

More than 14 million dengue cases were reported to the World Health Organization in 2024. That was more than double the previous annual record.

The longest view comes from a 2010 analysis in the journal Nature. Around 1900, malaria was a constant presence across an estimated 58 percent of the world’s land. By 2007, that figure had fallen to 30 percent.

Within the smaller area, far fewer people carried the parasite. In Africa, the share of people infected with the deadliest malaria parasite fell from 40 percent in 1900 to 1929, to 24 percent in 2010 to 2015.

Europe shows the same pattern. Malaria began to fade there in the 1800s while temperatures were rising, and the review credits better living conditions, better health care, and improved irrigation and drainage.

Cold didn’t stop the disease, either. Malaria was common in the marshy parishes of England from the 16th to the 18th centuries, during an especially cold period. In Finland, mosquitoes survived freezing winters by sheltering inside houses and stables.

The two diseases differ first in the tools people built to fight them. Efforts to wipe out malaria go back to the 1950s, with house spraying and mass drug campaigns, and later with bed nets treated with insecticide.

Global spending on malaria rose from $1.5 billion to $4.3 billion between 2000 and 2016. Over that time, the yearly rate of new cases fell 36 percent and the death rate fell 60 percent.

“What started as a climate story – the impact of temperature, rainfall and humidity on malaria and dengue – became a human story,” Cumming told Earth.com.

Dengue had no matching toolkit. There are still no drugs that treat the virus, vaccines are only now arriving, and bed nets do little against mosquitoes that bite in daylight.

And mosquitoes are building resistance to insecticides, a problem the authors flag for both diseases.

Cities are the second difference. Aedes mosquitoes lay eggs in gutters and water containers, and cities offer plenty of those, along with lots of people to bite.

Anopheles prefer more natural pools, which cities tend to lack, so urban growth tends to cut malaria.

The authors flag one important exception. Anopheles stephensi, a malaria mosquito from Asia that breeds in artificial containers, has now settled into cities in the Horn of Africa.

The good news on malaria has slowed. New cases per 1,000 people at risk fell from 79 in 2000 to 59 in 2015. By 2024, the figure was back up to 64.

In 18 countries, the rate rose by at least 70 percent between 2015 and 2024. Resistance to key malaria drugs is a growing concern too.

Extreme weather can undo progress quickly. After record flooding hit Pakistan in 2022, malaria cases there rose fivefold.

Asked what should be done first to curb the spread, Cumming said the answer is a renewed commitment from the global community to fight malaria and dengue.

That includes scaling up public health measures that work and speeding up research on new ways to prevent and treat both diseases.

The forecasts don’t agree on malaria. Some models predict it will spread into new areas as the planet warms. Others predict that hotter, drier conditions will shrink it in parts of Africa where it’s common today.

Adding wealth to a model changes the picture. Using climate alone, one model estimated that 5.2 billion people would be at risk of malaria by 2050. When it also counted each country’s economic output per person, the estimate dropped to 2.0 billion.

Much of the temperature evidence also comes from lab studies that were never checked in the field. And mosquitoes may adapt to local heat, which fixed temperature rules can’t capture.

For dengue, newer tools are arriving. One vaccine has been licensed in many countries since 2022. The World Mosquito Program, a not-for-profit group owned by Monash University, releases mosquitoes carrying a bacterium called Wolbachia, which makes them less able to spread the virus.

It’s still unclear whether those tools can do for dengue what bed nets and drugs did for malaria. In order to find out, researchers will need years of dengue case records from the places that use them.

The full study was published in the journal Science Advances.

The Benefits of Biking for Seniors, Including the Mental and Physical Payoffs

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

You might find a few surprising advantages of cycling on this list.

Gear-obsessed editors choose every product we review. We may earn commission if you buy from a link. How we test gear.

We know you think we’re going to tell you all the ways cycling improves your heart health and muscle strength—and it does, but guess what? According to a 2023 scoping review in Frontiers in Sports and Active Living, studies show that when older adults cycle regularly, their happiness improves and they are better able to maintain their quality of life.

In fact, even though many people associate aging with physical and mental declines, regular exercise—like a daily bike ride—can help slow or even reverse some of these effects.

“Regular physical activity helps seniors maintain good health, improve their quality of life, and increase their overall well-being,” Mary Sabat, MS, RDN, a personal trainer and dietitian in Georgia tells Bicycling. “It can enhance physical strength, flexibility, and endurance, making it easier to perform daily activities and maintain independence.”

Those are just few reasons why biking for seniors is so great—but here are all the advantages you gain when you start pedaling regularly.

While almost forms of physical activity improve physical and mental health, cycling is particularly well-suited for those in their golden years due to its accessibility. After all, cycling is easy on the joints, even for those with joint pain or injuries. “It can be tailored to individual fitness levels and adjusted to accommodate different abilities and physical limitations, too,” says Sabat. “Recumbent bikes, trikes, or electric-assist bicycles can be used to make cycling more accessible for those with mobility challenges or balance issues.”

Robert Patterson, a cyclist and lawyer, has been cycling for more than 40 years. He points out all the obvious aerobic and strength-building gains he’s enjoyed over the years, but also the psychological benefits. “Cycling also benefits my mental health. My attitude and overall focus are always improved by being on the bike,” he says.

Here are nine reasons, according to the experts, for you to saddle up and hit the road (or the gym) for a ride.

Aerobic exercise is the key to managing heart health. Because the heart is a muscle, it needs to be exercised on a regular basis to maintain its strength and optimal function. And because the heart and lungs work together with the venous system to deliver oxygenated blood to your muscles, any exercise that increases your heart rate and breath rate for an extended period of time will help improve your heart health and overall cardiovascular function.

Plus, as anyone who has ever pushed the pedals and pumped through a 20- or 30-minute bike ride knows, cycling will definitely get your heart rate up. The repetitive cycling motion qualifies as a moderate to intense form of aerobic exercise that you can easily adapt to your personal fitness level. This means that even if you haven’t exercised in years, by starting at a lower intensity and making adjustments to your workouts based on your heart rate, you can safely and effectively add cycling to your weekly routine.

“As individuals age, the risk of heart disease increases,” says Sabat. “Cycling is a low-impact aerobic exercise that helps improve cardiovascular fitness, strengthens the heart muscle, and enhances blood circulation. It can lower the risk of heart attacks, strokes, and high blood pressure, thereby promoting a healthy heart.”

In fact, a series of two 2019 review studies (part 1 and part 2) published in the British Journal of Sports Medicine found that all forms of cycling reduce the risk of cardiovascular disease and also reduced the risk factor profile for poor heart health—meaning that cyclists had better body composition stats, blood lipid profiles, cardiorespiratory fitness levels, and more time spent exercising. All of these together help mitigate the risk for developing heart disease.

You can combat practically any chronic condition, including heart disease, along with type 2 diabetes, arthritis, and cancer with a regular ride. Exercise helps keep the blood flowing, systems moving, and cells turning over in a way that promotes overall health. For instance, high-intensity exercise (and high-intensity cycling, specifically) helps improve blood glucose response, according to research, which may reduce the risk of type 2 diabetes.

Even in the case of cancer, participating in a regular cycling routine has been shown to reduce the risk. For example, May 2020 study published in The Lancet found an association between participating in a daily bike commute and a 16 percent decrease in cancer mortality and an 11 percent decrease in cancer incidence.

“By incorporating a ride here and there into their routine, seniors can reduce the risk of chronic conditions. These conditions can be debilitating, but with regular exercise, seniors can take proactive steps toward prevention and management,” Josh Weight, a certified sports physiotherapist and the director of Gravity Physio in Australia tells Bicycling.

It’s not unusual for seniors to experience regular joint pain associated with arthritis or previous injuries. In fact, according to the National Poll on Healthy Aging, about 70 percent of older adults report current joint pain. This can be exacerbated by extended periods spent sitting, or simply not moving around much, as sedentary behavior is directly associated with increased incidences of knee and other joint pain.

While on the surface this may not seem like a big deal, it can lead to a cascading series of events where joint pain or arthritis contributes to a reduced range of motion. This reduced range of motion can influence balance, coordination, and even the ability to perform everyday tasks. These issues can also contribute to a reduced quality of life and even an increased risk of falls. As such, any activity that can be done without contributing to joint pain while protecting and strengthening the structures that help stabilize the joints (muscles, tendons, and ligaments) is an important undertaking for older adults’ overall health and well-being.

“Cycling is a low-impact activity that puts minimal stress on the joints, making it an excellent option for individuals with joint-related issues. It provides a cardiovascular workout without placing excessive strain on the knees, hips, or ankles,” Sabat says.

Whether done on an indoor bike or a more traditional road or mountain bike, one thing’s for sure: Cycling works the legs. It takes muscular strength, power, and endurance to keep the pedals moving, especially when climbing a hill or ramping up the resistance. But it’s not just the legs and glutes that benefit—cycling can help build strength in the shoulders, upper back, and core, as well.

Of course, you may not be looking to build massive quads as one of your long-term fitness goals, but developing muscular strength isn’t just about looking strong—it’s about maintaining your quality of life.

Research estimates muscle mass begins to decline by 3 to 8 percent every decade following your 30th birthday, with those rates increasing further once you hit 60. This age-related muscular decline over time makes it more challenging to continue doing everyday activities like climbing stairs or carrying groceries. It’s important to participate in exercises, like cycling, that will help maintain or build muscle mass over time to safeguard your ability to perform these daily activities.

In fact, according to a 2015 review study published in the European Review of Aging and Physical Activity, adults over 70 years of age experienced increased muscular strength and power when participating in a cycling exercise program at least three times a week for 12 to 16 weeks. This data flies in the face of anyone who believes they can’t develop muscle or strength as they age—it’s not only possible, it’s critical to overall health and functional ability.

One area in which cycling can be particularly beneficial is balance and coordination. This has ties to muscular strength, as strong, trained muscles tend to perform and react more quickly to stimuli than those that aren’t worked regularly. However, it also has to do with the unique requirements of cycling itself.

“Cycling requires balance and coordination to stay upright and navigate,” says Andrew White, a personal trainer and owner of Garage Gym Pro. “This can be especially beneficial for seniors as these abilities tend to decline with age, increasing the risk of falls and injuries.”

This isn’t just conjecture—a 2018 prospective study published in the Journal of Aging and Physical Activity found that older adults (between the ages of 65 and 85) who cycled daily had better balance, a faster pace while walking, and better self-reported lower-extremity function compared to those who didn’t bike regularly. Based on this study, it appears that cycling helps preserve balance and coordination which contributes to lower-body function that can help prevent falls.

Just as muscle mass tends to decline with age, an associated change in body composition takes place, which often leads to increased weight gain. And as weight creeps up, so do the risks for heart disease and chronic illness, particularly in populations considered obese.

Cycling and other forms of exercise can help keep weight and other disease risk factors in check. This is partly due to the muscle-protective benefits exercise offers, but also due to the increased calorie burn that occurs during moderate- to high-intensity exercise like cycling.

“Cycling can help seniors maintain a healthy body weight, reduce excess body fat, and prevent obesity-related health issues,” Sabat emphasizes.

It’s not unusual for seniors to experience social isolation that can contribute to physical and mental health decline. Changes in health, mobility, or driving status can keep seniors alone at home. This is unfortunate because strong social connections are positively associated with longevity.

Cycling can provide seniors with an accessible way to get out and about, and to also engage with other people of all ages. “Cycling can be an enjoyable and social activity for seniors. Exploring outdoor trails, participating in group rides, or engaging in community cycling events can foster a sense of camaraderie and connection with others,” says Weight.

And for seniors who want to start cycling, but don’t know how, community cycling clubs offer a great way to learn and connect at the same time.

In addition to the benefits of social interaction, cycling can boost mental health in a number of ways, especially if you’re riding your bike outside. “Cycling outdoors provides an opportunity to connect with nature, which can be therapeutic and stress-relieving,” says White.

In fact, according to a 2022 study published in the Journal of Affective Disorders, individuals who participated in outdoor cycling (or other outdoor activities like walking, jogging, or team sports), were less likely to experience depression than those who were inactive. Likewise, there’s evidence that even a single outdoor exercise session (like cycling) can impact mood, more than indoor exercise equivalents.

Even in the absence of an outdoor setting, physical activity as a whole is good for mental health. “Regular exercise releases endorphins which can elevate mood and reduce symptoms of anxiety and depression,” Sabat explains.

One final benefit of cycling for seniors: It can support cognitive function. “Studies have shown that physical activity can have positive effects on cognition and may help reduce the risk of cognitive decline and dementia in older adults. Exercise promotes better blood flow to the brain, stimulates the release of hormones that support brain health, and improves memory and attention,” Sabat shares.

Laura Williams, M.S., ACSM EP-C holds a master's degree in exercise and sport science and is a certified exercise physiologist through the American College of Sports Medicine. She also holds sports nutritionist, youth fitness, sports conditioning, and behavioral change specialist certifications through the American Council on Exercise. She has been writing on health, fitness, and wellness for 12 years, with bylines appearing online and in print for Men's Health, Healthline, Verywell Fit, The Healthy, Giddy, Thrillist, Men's Journal, Reader's Digest, and Runner's World. After losing her first husband to cancer in 2018, she moved to Costa Rica to use surfing, beach running, and horseback riding as part of her healing process. There, she met her current husband, had her son, and now splits time between Texas and Costa Rica.

Hard Rock Hotels Plug Into Accor Without Selling a Stake

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Enter your email for a complimentary article + exclusive offers.

Hard Rock keeps its equity and hands Accor the task of developing its pipeline of non-casino hotels. Accor collects ongoing fees and gets a well-known brand to essentially rent out to its loyalty members.

Earlier this year, Hard Rock International listed its hotel in New York City on Accor’s booking site and app and told almost no one.

The first month brought 84 room nights. By July, the channel was delivering more than 1,500 a month, more than Hard Rock’s own site produced for the hotel, said Jeff Hook, chief operating officer of Hard Rock. More than 90% of those guests were new to Hard Rock.

The New York hotel test was a prelude. On Monday, Accor and Hard Rock announced a global partnership. Hard Rock’s hotels will join ALL, Accor’s loyalty program. Ennismore, Accor's lifestyle joint venture, will help develop Hard Rock hotels worldwide, and Accor's global sales team will help Hard Rock win group and meetings business.

Hard Rock will have about 25 standalone hotels once the integration is fully underway, Hook said. The company also has about 10 casino hotels and three of them might eventually be listed on Accor, too.

Accor and Hard Rock International announced a global partnership on Monday that puts Hard Rock's hotels into Accor's ALL loyalty program, gives Hard Rock access to Accor's global sales team for group and meetings business, and enlists Ennismore, Accor's lifestyle joint venture, to help develop Hard Rock hotels worldwide. No equity changes hands. Accor isn't investing capital, and Chief Commercial, Digital, and Tech Officer Alix Boulnois described the structure as closer to a franchise model in which Accor is paid for the business it brings. The deal follows a quiet test: Hard Rock listed its New York hotel on Accor's site and app earlier this year, and the channel grew from 84 room nights in the first month to more than 1,500 a month by July — more than Hard Rock's own site produced for that hotel, with more than 90% of those guests new to Hard Rock, said Hard Rock COO Jeff Hook. Hook said bookings from Accor's channel are "substantially less expensive" than those from Expedia, Booking.com, or other online travel agencies. Hard Rock will have about 25 standalone hotels in the arrangement, talked to four large hotel companies before choosing Accor, and turned down offers to buy all or part of its hotel division. Ennismore's operational role will vary deal by deal, from finding development deals to full management, with casinos excluded.

The Bestselling Protein Powder on Amazon Is Also Our Budget-Friendly Pick

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

We’ve been using Optimum Nutrition Gold Standard 100% Whey for years. Here’s what you need to know.

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

THERE’S NEVER BEEN a better time to focus on your protein intake, with an arsenal of products ready to help you meet your macros. When tossing back chicken breast or sirloin isn't an option, protein powder is a valuable tool, especially when it comes to building lean muscle and promoting recovery. Unfortunately, most protein powders aren't cheap.

It's fair to be wary of budget options, especially if you use protein powder regularly. But there's an inexpensive protein powder that's an Amazon bestseller that's worth considering. It's called Optimum Nutrition Gold Standard 100% Whey, and we keep coming back to it. While it's not the cheapest protein powder you can find, the brand does a solid job balancing out nutritional needs and quality at a price tag most people can afford.

No protein powder is perfect, including this one. But with nearly 81,000 five-star reviews to back it up, plus an Informed Choice certification, you have to wonder if there's something to Optimum Nutrition Gold Standard. Two Men's Health editors put this powder to the test over several months, and have largely positive things to say about it. Keep these in mind before adding this protein powder to your cart.

The low price is what brings most people to the table for Optimum Nutrition Gold Standard 100% Whey. While price per serving varies depending on the size you buy, you're looking at $1.06 to $1.54 per serving, with more savings the larger the container you purchase.

By comparison, our best overall protein powder, Transparent Labs Grass-Fed Whey Protein Isolate, is $2 per serving. Should price be the main reason you choose a protein powder? Definitely not. But if you're able to get what you're looking for in an inexpensive powder, why wouldn't you go with it?

"The value is something to give props to," says MH Fitness and Reviews Editor Charles Thorp, NASM. The brand is able to keep the cost down because this isn't a pure whey protein isolate, Thorp explains. Instead, it's a blend of whey protein isolate, whey protein concentrate, and hydrolyzed whey protein. Still, that delivers 24g of protein and 5.5g of BCAAs per serving.

Texture matters with protein powder, and some can lean toward a sandy or gritty feel. That usually means it won't blend as well in your shake or smoothie, which can make it tough to keep using a product. But Optimum Nutrition has a light texture that blends easily.

"It’s fluffy, not sandy, like some protein powders," says MH Deputy Editor Paul Kita. "That tells me it’s going to blend well and won’t sit heavy in my gut." Thorp also praises Optimum Nutrition's texture. "The powder itself is lighter than the majority of other protein powders, which is nice because it opens up your options for mixers," he says. (Powders with a gritty or sandy texture don't usually mix well with water or thinner liquids.)

SHOP OPTIMUM NUTRITION WHEY PROTEIN POWDER

Optimum Nutrition has an impressive lineup of 24 flavors, ranging from basic options like chocolate and vanilla to things like banana cream. There are 10 different chocolate flavor combinations alone, giving you plenty to choose from.

"I’m a huge fan of the chocolate flavor," Kita says. "It’s not too sweet—a major protein powder deal-breaker for me." Kita finds it blends equally well in berry, banana, or black cherry smoothies. Thorp says the milk chocolate taste is "light" and agrees it works well with a range of smoothies and mixers. "The taste isn't overpowering at all, which is nice as it allows you to build your taste from the other real foods you like," he says.

It doesn't hurt to remind everyone that the Food and Drug Administration (FDA) doesn't really regulate supplements, including protein powder. As a result, what the label says doesn't always reflect what's inside the container. That makes third-party testing crucial. It verifies that you're actually getting what you think when you use a protein powder.

Optimum Nutrition is Informed-Choice Certified, which means it's regularly tested for unsafe and banned substances by LGC, an independent sports anti-doping lab. As a result, you can feel pretty solid reaching for this on a regular basis.

Thorp has been using Optimum Nutrition Gold Standard 100% Whey for years, including testing it more extensively over the past few months for this story. He regularly tried the powder blended into smoothies with peanut butter, banana, chia seeds, and coconut milk. He also had it occasionally mixed into whole milk.

Kita also tested the protein powder for months, blending it into smoothies with frozen fruit, chia seeds, and almond milk. During testing, MH editors took careful notes on elements like taste, texture, mixability, and value.

SHOP OPTIMUM NUTRITION WHEY PROTEIN POWDER

There is a trade-off to this powder's fluffy texture: It's not thick enough to mix with water. "For thicker protein powders, I like the ability to sometimes just mix water," Thorp says. "That isn't really an option with this one, which is one small detractor." (It did just fine in thicker consistency liquids like milk or smoothies, though.)

There's no outright harm when you have it in moderation, but it's worth noting that Optimum Nutrition relies on artificial sweeteners in its protein powder.

For most people, the blend of whey proteins isn't an issue. But if you have a sensitive stomach, it may be better to use a less dairy-heavy whey isolate instead.

If you like the idea of using whey protein and are OK with spending a little more, the Transparent Labs Grass-Fed Whey Protein Isolate is a MH favorite thanks to its clean ingredients, high protein content per serving, and wide range of flavor options.

For athletes, we dig Thorne Whey Protein Isolate. It's NSF-certified for professional athletes and blends well in water, milk, and smoothies.

There's an overwhelming number of protein powders to choose from out there. But if you want an inexpensive workhorse protein powder that'll help you meet your macros and actually contains what the label states, the Optimum Nutrition Gold Standard 100% Whey is worth your time.

Felix Nmecha’s faith is forcing Borussia Dortmund into an uncomfortable debate

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Midfielder’s spectacular strikes rescued a frustrating day, only for celebrations to reignite dialogue that has followed him through his career

It was just what they needed at just the right time. For a tricky first half and beyond, Elversberg had been a stone in Borussia Dortmund’s shoe, the promoted side not just keeping the home team at bay but keeping them honest, nourishing ambitions of their own. “The fatigue was clearly visible,” said the sporting director, Ole Book, to Sky after a week which had contained Der Klassiker in Munich and a hard-fought Champions League victory over Betis. “We were a bit too focused on managing the game, which was understandable.”

Enter Felix Nmecha. BVB have enjoyed a largely excellent start to the season, are unbeaten and level on points with Bayern Munich at the top; Bayern lead, unsurprisingly, thanks to their superior goal difference. It is a gap which has been widened by Dortmund being far from prolific, a facet which looked as if it might undercut their efforts to roll over their visitors on Saturday, especially in the absence of the injured centre-forward Serhou Guirassy.

Somebody was needed to break it open, and it was Nmecha. With two majestic sweeps of his right foot, two rockets from outside the area, he won the game for his team. His decisive moments of elevated quality pulled BVB away from their opponents when the game threatened to drift. “In terms of talent, qualities, and technique, he’s one of the best midfielders in the world. Add to that his finishing ability, and there aren’t many who are better,” Book beamed. That they go into the international break neck-and-neck with Bayern is all down to their No 8.

After scoring, Nmecha pointed to the sky, then mimed lifting a crown from his head and placing it on the ground. It was nothing new. He did the same, also in front of the Südtribune, the yawning, world-famous 24,000-capacity terrace in which much of the club’s hardcore support stand, after scoring against Paderborn in September. It was the same celebration that Nmecha used when he struck Germany’s opening goal of the World Cup against Curaçao.

The celebration is a nod to Ballers in God (BIG), the Christian group founded “to use the global platform of football to make Jesus known”, and in particular to their campaign The King’s Return. Yet this latest display provoked a response from BVB’s fan and support department. “When it comes to the values of our club,” it said in a statement the day after the Elversberg game, “discrimination, and the question of what kind of societal image of humanity is given space within and around Borussia Dortmund, we cannot and will not remain silent.”

The German public broadcaster ARD has linked BIG to ultra-conservative positions on issues including homosexuality and transgender identity. “Footballers are role models for millions of people, especially children and young people,” the fan and support department continued. “If this reach conveys content that excludes certain groups in society, we cannot dismiss this as merely private religious practice. Where religious beliefs are used to deny people their dignity or equality, our tolerance for intolerance ends.”

Reports suggest the club has been in dialogue with Nmecha since the Paderborn game, and a statement issued in response to Kicker’s inquiries said those talks would continue – while defending Nmecha. “For Borussia Dortmund, it is essential to take the concerns and needs of the fanbase seriously and to address them,” said the club. “Naturally, we also take the current media coverage and the associated questions and discussions very seriously.” Dortmund appear to have been careful not to use any images of the celebration on the club’s official social channels.

These issues and this dialogue have been almost a constant since Nmecha arrived in June 2023, as a de facto Jude Bellingham replacement. His faith had already been a key part of his social media messaging and, as his transfer from Wolfsburg neared completion, some fan groups voiced their opposition, pointing to Nmecha’s sharing of anti-trans posts by the American rightwinger Matt Walsh, among others. A bright yellow banner was hung outside Signal Iduna Park sprayed with the message “@BVB Steh zu deinen worten”, a message to the club to “stick by your words”. In June 2023 Nmecha posted: “I genuinely love ALL people and don’t discriminate against anyone.”

Dortmund’s motto, or marketing strapline, of Echte Liebe (true love), has been used in the last decade-plus to quantify exactly what makes the club special to audiences all over the world. What BVB believe elevates them is a connection with the fans, a mutual respect and understanding that goes way beyond what has increasingly become a company-customer relationship in modern times. Engaging with supporter concerns is important everywhere in German football and, arguably at Dortmund, there is a commercial imperative to visibly do so.

It was widely reported at the time of his arrival that Nmecha had a clause written into his contract that gave Dortmund the power to fine him if he made social media posts which they felt contradicted the club’s values. The club again had words with the player in 2025 after he posted a message of condolence to the family of Charlie Kirk (Nmecha deleted another post which said Kirk had “peacefully stood up for his beliefs and values”).

Hamburg 3-1 Mönchengladbach, Mainz 1-2 Bayern, Paderborn 2-2 Eintracht Frankfurt, RB Leipzig 5-2 Augsburg, Dortmund 2-1 SV Elversberg, VfB Stuttgart 1-0 Werder, Köln 1-2 Leverkusen, Freiburg 2-0 Union Berlin, Hoffenheim 2-1 Schalke

The difference now is that Nmecha, after a difficult start in Westfalen, has become one of Niko Kovac’s key players, and one of the most recognised in his position in Europe. He scored the first goal of Jürgen Klopp’s Germany tenure, and was linked with big-money moves to the Premier League last summer. So BVB must protect their present, in the sporting sense, their future, in the financial sense, and their very fabric, in terms of their continued relationship with supporters, already strained in recent years by their deal with the arms manufacturer Rheinmetall. For his part Nmecha has responded enigmatically and characteristically, quoting a Bible verse on his socials as a caption to snaps of the game. The one thing the club cannot say is that they never saw this coming.

Bayern remain on top but nothing is easy at the moment, even after their brilliant victory at Atlético in midweek. They closed out a narrow in at Mainz but Manuel Neuer was injured again, suffering a partial ligament tear of his right foot on the day he became Bayern’s oldest Bundesliga player at 40 years and 225 days, his sixth substitution in 2026. Whether he will – or should – return is now a fair question, with Jonas Urbig a ready-made replacement. Harry Kane provided assists for both goals to reach double figures in the category, his best-ever assist return in a season already.

The mood was not helped, however, by Uli Hoeness’ latest comments on Jamal Musiala, throwing doubt on whether he can recover his best level on the programme Der Sonntags-Stammtisch. “He’s still getting paid,” the honorary president underlined. “But imagine if FC Bayern were to say what he’s capable of now isn’t what’s stipulated in the contract?”

Even if there was another combination of the El Mala brothers – Malek setting up a goal for Saïd this week – for Köln in the derby with Leverkusen, the game had already been decided then, by a typically classy intervention by Patrik Schick.

The Most AI-Paranoid States in the US Revealed

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

The Most AI-Paranoid States in the US Revealed

Wyoming is the most AI-paranoid state in the U.S., recording 4,700 annual searches per 100,000 residents. Vermont ranks second with 4,300, followed by Alaska with 3,600.

The experts at VIP-Grinders analyzed 47 AI-related search terms using Google Ads Keyword Planner, covering topics including job security, privacy, healthcare, and AI safety. Across the country, the terms generate an estimated 1.13 million searches annually, reflecting a mix of curiosity and concern about the technology.

Wyoming tops the ranking with 4,681 annual AI-related searches per 100,000 residents, narrowly ahead of Vermont’s 4,292. Despite their relatively small populations, the two states generate 27,000 and 27,600 searches a year, respectively.

Alaska ranks third with 3,599.7 annual searches per 100,000 residents, followed by North Dakota (3,466) and South Dakota (3,113). Delaware (2,727) takes sixth place, while Rhode Island, Montana, Maine, and New Hampshire complete the top 10.

Population has a significant impact on the rankings. California generates the most AI-related searches overall, with 464,400 annually, but ranks 25th when adjusted for population, at 1,175 searches per 100,000 residents. New York and Texas each record 308,400 annual searches, but rank 13th and 28th, respectively, with rates of 1,527 and 1,058 per 100,000 residents.

Healthcare is the biggest source of AI-related concern, with the term “AI in healthcare” generating 21,400 searches a month, or more than 250,000 annually. Fears about an AI takeover of the world rank second, attracting nearly three times less search interest.

Concerns about AI taking over the world also appear to outweigh questions about its impact on employment. The phrase “will AI take my job” receives nearly five times fewer searches than “will AI take over the world” annually. Employment remains a recurring concern, however, with questions about AI replacing doctors and programmers generating a combined 21,600 searches a year.

The term “superintelligence,” which has recently entered wider public discussion, also features in the data, attracting 45,600 searches annually. However, it remains a relatively niche topic, with twice as many searches for “will AI take over the world.”

NYSE to settle tokenized stocks on Avalanche in planned 24/7 market

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

NYSE says the venue will support equities issued on multiple blockchains, while Avalanche will underpin the ledger used to record and settle trades.

The New York Stock Exchange has selected Avalanche blockchain for clearing and settlement on its planned 24/7 tokenized-securities platform, Ava Labs said in a statement on Monday.

The platform is being designed to support around-the-clock trading, clearing, and settlement of tokenized securities, pending regulatory approval. It is targeting a fourth-quarter launch and will use NYSE’s existing technology to match trades, according to The Wall Street Journal.

"It will support the platform's 24/7 atomic settlement needs with dedicated infrastructure with predictable performance and costs, the control to meet its regulatory and operational requirements, and connectivity to a broader ecosystem of assets and liquidity," Ava Labs CEO Emin Gün Sirer said in the statement.

The selection follows about a year of testing. Ava Labs President Charley Cooper told The Block in September that NYSE had spent the previous year testing Avalanche and working with the company on how the technology could fit into its systems.

Cooper said NYSE had also examined the project’s economics and technology, but stopped short of confirming a selection. ICE Head of Strategic Initiatives Michael Blaugrund said at the time that the exchange operator was "very engaged" with Avalanche.

NYSE Head of Digital Issuance Dennis Lee said the exchange is evaluating different technologies for different parts of the platform. The venue will support equities listed on a range of blockchains, while its settlement ledger needs to provide speed, reliability, predictable costs, and control over who can participate, he said.

The exchange has also enlisted more partners supporting the venue. Last week, it announced agreements with Broadridge Financial Solutions, Computershare, Equiniti, and Superstate to support issuer-sponsored tokenized securities and transfer-agent functions. Securitize and tZERO were already listed as partners.

$4.5 Trillion Nvidia Investigating CEO’s Affair With an Alleged Chinese Spy

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

$4.5 Trillion Nvidia Investigating CEO’s Affair With an Alleged Chinese Spy

Nvidia, a $4.5 trillion company at the time of writing, has a new CEO scandal. Nvidia said on Sunday that its board is investigating whether its CEO, Jensen Huang, had been targeted by foreign intelligence as part of an extramarital affair.

Huang admitted to the affair in a public statement to Nvidia employees.

“I owe my family and all of you an apology,” the 63-year-old CEO said. “I was unfaithful to my wife. My actions hurt her and our children. There is no excuse for what I did. I am deeply sorry.

“Throughout my life, my family has given me love and support. They deserve my honesty and loyalty. Instead, I let them down and damaged the life we built together. I take full responsibility for what I did and the hurt I caused, and I accept the criticism that comes with it.”

Huang continued, addressing his wife and family in a public statement on Nvidia’s website, before turning his attention to the company he has led for over three decades, saying the company’s “board, partners, and shareholders” deserve “better” from him.

“Leading NVIDIA is the privilege of my life. My responsibility is to serve you and be worthy of your trust,” Huang writes. “I have fallen short of that responsibility. I am fully committed to NVIDIA and will work to earn back your confidence.”

While people certainly have plenty of beliefs surrounding extramarital affairs and breaches of trust in personal settings, Huang’s admitted affair potentially takes on a much bigger meaning. Nvidia’s board said it is investigating Huang’s affair to determine if Huang’s affair partner, Alice Huang (unrelated), is a foreign intelligence operative passing vital information to China.

While this story escalated dramatically on Sunday when Huang admitted to an affair and Nvidia said it was launching an investigation, The New York Times reported about Jensen and Alice Huang’s close relationship late last month, adding that U.S. authorities had investigated Alice Huang and the company she used to work for, Singapore-based Megaspeed. U.S. authorities determined that Alice Huang was a suspected Chinese intelligence asset.

Another New York Times report alleges that Alice Huang was recruited by top Chinese agencies to get information on Jensen Huang to exert pressure on the company to supply its best AI chips to Chinese companies, which would, of course, have huge implications for the global AI race and the techno-geopolitical landscape.

As for Nvidia’s investigation, the company says it is working with independent outside counsel to examine Huang’s actions.

“The NVIDIA Board is initiating an ​independent investigation in light of recent reporting that founder and CEO Jensen Huang has been the target of a foreign intelligence effort. A committee of independent directors, assisted by outside independent counsel, will oversee the investigation,” Nvidia says.

Nvidia and Jensen Huang are very popular in the White House, and unsurprisingly, President Donald Trump had something to say.

“I have not spoken to him ​about it. I just know he’s a ​fine person, but you never know what is going on. I hope everything’s fine,” Trump told reporters yesterday, November 8. “I think he’s an honorable guy.”

Trump said that Huang had done “amazing” work for the United States and added, “I think he probably has an excuse for it.”

This is simply massive news in the technology industry and could have far-reaching impacts. As investor Gary Black tells Reuters, this casts serious doubt on Huang’s ability to lead Nvidia following the probe. There’s no obvious succession plan inside the company, either.

Others don’t think Huang will be replaced anyway, with many citing concerns about Nvidia’s valuation if he were removed from his leadership position.

“It would be an absolute bloodbath,” says Chris Weston, Head of Research at Pepperstone.

Nvidia’s stock dropped nearly 6% in pre-market trading.

“If it is found out that she’s confirmed as a spy, then, crikey, it doesn’t go well,” Weston adds.

“I am hoping for a simple affair, not a risk to our national security,” Bill Ackman, Pershing Square CEO, told Reuters.

Ahead of its investigation, Nvidia itself says it has no reason to believe that Huang’s affair compromised the company or U.S. national security interests.

Ori Creator Is Very Unhappy With How Nintendo Handled Zelda: Ocarina of Time Remake

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Covering the video games industry since 2017, with experience in news, articles, and reviews. Currently a writer at DualShockers and gaming magazines.

We all know that few games have left as lasting an impression on the gaming industry as The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time. Even decades after its original release, the Nintendo classic continues to inspire developers and entire generations of players.

And its recently released remake has already brought plenty of attention back to Hyrule, with many critics and players praising its beautiful new presentation and sharing emotional reactions to revisiting one of their favorite adventures – no wonder it’s already one of the highest-rated games ever!

However, not everyone is completely satisfied with Nintendo's approach. Thomas Mahler, best known as the creator of the acclaimed Ori franchise, has shared a rather lengthy criticism of the remake on X (formerly Twitter), explaining why he believes the developer missed an opportunity to make the experience much better from a game design perspective.

Mahler recently shared his thoughts on social media after playing the Ocarina of Time remake alongside his son, who was immediately impressed by its new art direction. The developer even mentioned that his son was so amazed by the visuals that he tried touching the screen to grab the Kokiri Sword when he first obtained it, which is pretty cute!

The remake's dev team included younger folks, several of whom never played the original Ocarina of Time, who provided a much-needed modern view of things.

Unfortunately, Mahler himself wasn't nearly as impressed with the experience. In his post, he curiously explained that he has always been somewhat disappointed by the original Ocarina of Time, particularly when comparing it to A Link to the Past. For him, the Nintendo 64 adventure feels emptier and more confusing, and the problem now is that the remake hasn't addressed those issues.

Thomas Mahler, best known as the creator of the acclaimed Ori franchise, has shared a rather lengthy criticism of the remake, explaining why he believes the developer missed an opportunity to make the experience much better from a game design perspective.

His biggest complaint revolves around Hyrule Field, which he considers one of the game's weakest areas. Mahler believes its relatively empty design was understandable back in 1998, considering the Nintendo 64's technical limitations. However, with modern hardware and the technology available today, he feels Nintendo could have done much more than simply improve its appearance. “Sure, Hyrule Field looks prettier now, but it's still just as empty and uninteresting as it was back then,” Mahler explained.

Mahler believes the remake could have taken a similar approach to A Link to the Past by introducing more activities, hidden areas, and interactions throughout Hyrule Field. He also suggested that acquiring new dungeon items should encourage players to revisit previously explored locations and discover paths or secrets they couldn't access before.

Instead, he feels Nintendo preserved an outdated design that could have benefited from a more substantial overhaul. He even argued that the game would deserve a 7.5 or 8 out of 10 today, rather than the perfect scores some reviewers have awarded it.

Eiji Aonuma and the development team for the Ocarina of Time remake discussed how one of the original's infamous dungeons is being improved.

Naturally, Mahler's comments didn't go unnoticed, and plenty of Zelda fans pushed back against his opinion. Some questioned whether changing the original game's structure would undermine what made Ocarina of Time so special in the first place.

The developer wasn't particularly convinced by those arguments, however, and continued defending his position in the replies. “Let's be a bunch of bozos instead who praise 1998 game design and act like it's the end all be all even though Nintendo themselves would never ever design a shit overworld like that for their next mainline entry," he responded.

It's worth noting that Mahler did acknowledge the remake's impressive visuals, and his criticism is primarily directed at its level design rather than its presentation. Still, his comments highlight an interesting discussion about how much developers should change when revisiting beloved classics.

After all, preserving what made a game special and improving the elements that haven't aged quite as well aren't necessarily mutually exclusive. And while plenty of players are clearly enjoying their return to Hyrule, Mahler's reaction shows that even a legendary adventure like Ocarina of Time won't be everyone's cup of tea.

The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time is one of the most beloved games of all time, but these remakes might be just as meaningful to fans.

Does Running Build Muscle?

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

You know running builds endurance. Here’s how it can change your body composition.

Running obviously builds your endurance. While one mile (or five miles) might feel really hard when you first start running, those distances become much easier the more you get out there.

Running also builds strength. As you power up hills or hit a finishing kick, you realize you have the force required to pick up the pace.

But does running build muscle? Can you change your body composition simply by clocking more miles?

The short answer is yes. But there’s more to the story. Here’s what to know about running and its affect on muscle mass.

Research shows running can produce muscle growth. For example, a 2015 research review, published in the journal Exercise and Sport Sciences Reviews, found that aerobic exercise can increase skeletal muscle and whole muscle size, particularly in sedentary individuals. (This means those just starting to run are more likely to experience the muscle-building benefits.) Those study authors suggest aerobic exercise as a mechanism to stave off the muscle loss that comes with aging.

Likewise, a 2017 study published in the International Journal of Exercise Science found that running induced muscle growth in the vastus lateralis muscle (one of the quadricep muscles) in 12 recreationally active university students. The participants completed about 25 sessions of high-intensity running (four sets of four minutes of running at 90 to 95 percent of heart rate max, with three minutes of active rest in between) for 10 weeks.

This muscle growth likely stems from the way running loads the body—more specifically, the lower body. “With running, the majority of the muscle work or contraction is eccentric, which is the hardest load on the body,” Joe McConkey, a Boston-based exercise physiologist and USATF-certified running coach tells Runner’s World. Eccentric contractions occur when a muscle lengthens under load or tension. For example, during the lowering phase of a squat, the quadriceps (front of thigh) muscles eccentrically contract.

When running, “the act of landing, where you’re absorbing two to four times your body weight, is done eccentrically. That’s the major stimulus for muscle growth during running, particularly for beginners,” McConkey says.

Recent research shows that aerobic exercise may have more of an effect on maintaining muscle mass, rather than building it. That’s especially true if you stick to a similar routine, without adding intensity.

That said, new runners, compared to those who have been running for a while, are more likely to see the most noticeable changes in muscle mass and strength just by virtue of running more often or for longer for the first time (or the first time in a long time).

Also, even though runners who consistently do steady-state runs won’t build more muscle mass, their muscles can start to look more defined as body composition changes due to increasing mileage, adds McConkey.

If you’ve been running a long time, you’ve also probably already built strength in the slow-twitch muscle fibers that primarily power your long distance running, Janet Hamilton, C.S.C.S., owner of Running Strong, tells Runner’s World.

All runners can develop more strength—in terms of power and muscle ability—and likely more muscle by tapping into their fast-twitch muscle fibers, which have less endurance than slow-twitch fibers but exert more force. “These don’t really get called upon until either a) there’s an activity that requires more strength than slow-twitch fibers can muster or b) the slow-twitch fibers are fatigued and you keep going,” says Hamilton.

To recruit more of those fast-twitch fibers, you need to do something that requires greater power output. Think about the massive leg muscles of a sprinter: “Any high-powered, short bout of exercise—so long as it is progressive in nature (i.e. harder, faster, higher than what you have already adapted to)—will increase the size of fast-twitch fibers, and thus increase muscle mass,” says McConkey.

In summary, to promote muscle growth, you need a bigger challenge on your muscles and a variety of challenges, otherwise running will offer more maintenance than progression.

To support your muscle-building efforts, consider the general exercise recommendations for each week: at least three cardio workouts, two strength workouts, and at least one flexibility workout. Incorporating these all into your routine will not only support your muscles, but make you a more well-rounded athlete.

One crucial component of that muscle-building plan is weight training. Strength workouts create micro-tears in your muscles that, during recovery, build back bigger and stronger. You also need enough protein in your diet to help with this process.

When it comes to running, though, an easy way to build fast-twitch fibers and muscle mass is through hill workouts. “Running up a hill requires more muscle power than running on level ground,” says Hamilton. You may not be lifting a weight, but you’re carrying your own weight up an incline against the force of gravity—its own kind of resistance training.

Speed workouts will also engage fast-twitch muscle fibers. “If you’ve been doing nothing but easy effort, marathon-pace runs, incorporating short to moderate intervals at a faster, 5K pace is going to require you to exert more force every time your foot hits the ground,” says Hamilton. “And that’s how you speed up—you generate a greater push-off.”

To build more muscle on your runs, check out these workouts below. The mileage, effort, and total distance will depend on your running fitness, but each workout can be scaled up or down to meet you where you are in your training.

Recommended Experience: No experience necessary! Anyone can accomplish this training plan.

Get the PlanRecommended Experience: This plan is for a new runner who is just getting back into exercise.

Get the PlanRecommended Experience: For a new runner who has been exercising regularly for at least a year, or someone who has completed a few 5Ks or 10Ks and can complete 6 miles in one workout. This plan peaks at 28 miles in a week with a 12-mile long run.

Get the PlanRecommended Experience: For runners comfortable exercising for at least an hour at a moderate effort. This plan peaks at 32 miles in a week with a 14-mile long run.

Get the PlanRecommended Experience: For a consistent runner used to regularly working out four to five times a week. The plan starts with a 10-mile long run and peaks at 44 to 47 miles a week with a 22-mile long run, helping you average under a 9:10 per mile come race day.

John Vasudevan, M.D. is an associate professor at the University of Pennsylvania. He is board-certified in Physical Medicine & Rehabilitation and Sports Medicine. He is a Team Physician for UPenn Athletics and  medical director of the Broad Street Run and Philadelphia Distance Run, and previously for the Rock 'n' Roll Half-Marathon and Tri-Rock Triathlon in Philadelphia. He is a director of the running and endurance Sports Medicine Program at Penn Medicine.  Dr. Vasudevan provides non-operative management of musculoskeletal conditions affecting athletes and active individuals of all levels, and combines injury rehabilitation with injury prevention. He utilizes a variety of ultrasound-guided procedures and regenerative approaches such as platelet-rich plasma and percutaneous ultrasonic tenotomy. He sees patients at the Penn Medicine and the Philadelphia Veterans Administration hospital. Dr. Vasudevan attended medical school at the University of Wisconsin School of Medicine and Public Health in Madison. After his Transitional Year in Tucson, Arizona, he went to residency in PM&R at Thomas Jefferson University in Philadelphia and onwards to Stanford University for his fellowship in Sports Medicine. He has been in practice at the University of Pennsylvania since 2012.

Acer Predator Helios Neo 16S AI Review: Slim OLED gaming that doesn't last long

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →
