Reviews, benchmarks, and updates on current GPUs.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The Acer Predator Helios Neo 16S AI mostly succeeds as a thin-and-light gaming laptop, offering solid RTX 5070 Ti performance, a beautiful 240 Hz OLED display, comfortable input devices, and plentiful ports. Its biggest weakness is ironically its battery life, which falls short of what you'd expect from a system focused on portability.

Why you can trust Tom's Hardware Our expert reviewers spend hours testing and comparing products and services so you can choose the best for you. Find out more about how we test.

Not everyone wants a hulking gaming laptop. Some prefer something stealthier. Acer’s Predator Helios Neo 16S AI aims for portability but still packs a Core Ultra 9 290HX Plus processor and Nvidia RTX 5070 Ti graphics into a sub-five-pound package. It also includes plenty of ports and a beautiful 240 Hz OLED display. That hardware gives it plenty to work with, but the question remains whether the Helios Neo 16S AI can still deliver solid gaming performance while being portable.

Acer’s obsidian black chassis maintains the look of the Predator series – angular, dark, and visible in the dark with its illuminated lid logo. At 14.05 x 10.85 x 0.74 inches and 4.85 pounds, it’s respectably trim for a gaming laptop, though the Gigabyte Aero X16 (14 x 9.9 x 0.78 inches, 4.2 pounds) and the HyperX Omen 15 (13.5 x 9.96 x 0.93 inches, 4.89 pounds) both have about an inch less depth since neither use a rear thermal shelf like Acer.

The build quality is decent – both the chassis and lid show little flex. The stamped aluminum panels on the lid bottom and chassis lid aren’t ultra-premium but still beat plastic. One downside is that the metal shows fingerprints easily, which are difficult to clean off.

Acer provides an impressive port variety across three sides: Thunderbolt 4, three USB-A 3.2, USB-C 3.2, HDMI 2.1, 2.5 Gbps Ethernet, a MicroSD card reader, and an audio jack. The rear ports conveniently leave cables out of sight.

Inside, the laptop offers Wi-Fi 6E, which isn’t as modern as it could be, though the Killer AX1675i card and accompanying Killer Ethernet jack offer simultaneous network connections via Killer Doubleshot Pro for gamers that want to ensure network priority for the games over one connection and all other apps over the other. It’s also notable that Acer includes a zippered, padded travel sleeve.

Nvidia GeForce RTX 5070 Ti (12GB GDDR7, 1,612 MHz boost clock, 115 W maximum graphics power)

16-inch, OLED, 16:10, 2560 x 1600, 240 Hz, G-Sync

Thunderbolt 4, 3x USB-A 3.2, USB-C 3.2, HDMI 2.1, Ethernet, MicroSD card reader, audio

14.05 x 10.85 x 0.74 inches (357 x 276 x 18.8 mm)

We tested the Predator Helios Neo 16S AI with a Core Ultra 9 290HX Plus processor, Nvidia RTX 5070 Ti graphics card, and 16GB of RAM.

Playing 007 First Light, I saw about 65 to 75 frames per second (FPS) in most scenes, with spikes to over 100 FPS in wide open areas. The game remained all-around playable, though it did require using DLSS to achieve these framerates at native 2560 x 1600 with High settings.

Get Tom's Hardware's best news and in-depth reviews, straight to your inbox.

Our comparison systems for this Acer are Gigabyte’s Aero X16 (Ryzen AI 7 350, RTX 5070 85W, $1,899.99 when tested, $1,499.99 as of this writing) and HyperX’s Omen 15 (Core Ultra 9 386H, RTX 5070 85W, $2,099.99 when tested, about $3,159.99 as of this writing).

Comparisons will focus on the 1920 x 1080 results, but we also included numbers at each laptop’s native resolution: 2560 x 1600 for the Acer and Gigabyte while the HP uses 2880 x 1800.

Overall, the Predator Helios Neo 16S AI performed significantly better than both the Gigabyte and HyperX laptops. Much of that has to do with its higher-wattage RTX 5070 Ti, though its Core Ultra 9 290HX Plus processor is also more modern.

In Shadow of the Tomb Raider (Highest detail preset), Acer’s 125 FPS at 1080p was on par with the HyperX (126 FPS), though it dominated at its 1600p native resolution, with 76 FPS against Gigabyte’s 60 FPS and HyperX’s 64 FPS.

Acer’s advantage continued in Far Cry 6 (Ultra), where it produced 122 FPS to Gigabyte’s 85 FPS and HyperX’s 107 FPS at native resolution. The lead narrowed in Red Dead Redemption 2 (Medium) where Acer and Gigabyte practically tied at 1080p and were within a few FPS at native (52 FPS for Acer and 49 FPS for Gigabyte). The HyperX remained behind.

The Acer also demonstrated markedly better performance in Cyberpunk 2077 (Ray Tracing Ultra) particularly at native resolution, where its 21 FPS outran Gigabyte (17 FPS) and HyperX (5 FPS).

In Forza Horizon 6 (Extreme), Acer’s 72 FPS at 1080p was strangely lower than HyperX’s 80 FPS, but the results flipped at native, where Acer’s 60 FPS was considerably ahead of HyperX’s 51 FPS.

While not a powerhouse, the Predator Helios Neo 16S AI demonstrates competent gaming performance at its 2560 x 1600 native resolution. Crucially, the extra depth from Acer’s thermal shelf design lets its GPU draw 115 W, considerably more than its Gigabyte and HyperX competitors

We test the sustained performance of gaming laptops running 15 loops of the Metro: Exodus stress test, simulating half an hour of gaming. During the test, the Predator Helios Neo 16S AI averaged 86.82 FPS across all runs. The variance was less than one FPS between runs, indicating stable thermal performance. The Core Ultra 9 290HX Plus ran at an average of 2.94 GHz on its P-cores, 2.16 GHz on its E-cores, and had an average temperature of 73 degrees Celsius. Meanwhile, the RTX 5070 Ti averaged 1.622 GHz and 74 C.

Our Predator Helios Neo 16S AI features a Core Ultra 9 290HX processor, 16GB of dual-channel DDR5-6400 memory, and a 1TB PCIe 4.0 SSD.

The Acer’s Core Ultra 9 290HX produced strong numbers in Geekbench. Its single-core score of 3,349 and multi-core score of 19,313 were well ahead of the Ryzen AI 7 350-powered Gigabyte (2,916 and 14,074) and Core Ultra 9 386H-powered HyperX (2,857 and 15,916). This continued into our 4K-to-1080p Handbrake transcoding testing, which Acer dominated with 2 minutes and 30 seconds. The Gigabyte (3:54) and HyperX (3:39) weren’t even close.

Acer also produced solid numbers in our 25GB file transfer test, averaging 2,034.84 MBps next to Gigabyte’s1,480.57 MBps and HyperX’s far slower 999.57 MBps.

Overall, this Acer is very capable for almost any kind of use. Multitasking will be limited by 16GB of RAM, but Acer sells versions of this laptop with 32GB and it can be upgraded after purchase.

Once glance at this Acer’s display leaves no doubt it’s a key selling point. Its OLED panel technology creates exceptionally vivid colors matched by excellent brightness, and its glossy surface gives it an almost liquid appearance. The 2560 x 1600 resolution is a good balance between detail and what the system’s RTX 5070 Ti graphics card can drive in today’s games. Gamers will also appreciate the smooth 240 Hz refresh rate and Nvidia G-Sync support. (As we’ll see in the battery life section, the screen only supports 240 Hz, resulting in higher-than-expected power consumption.)

Watching nature documentaries on National Geographic, I noted the vibrant colors of tropical scenes and the pitch blacks of the depths of the sea. Gaming was equally immersive – 007 First Light felt supremely fluid and looked sharp. I could even make out the writing on character’s uniform badges without zooming.

The Acer’s display covered a healthy 82.7% of the DCI-P3 color gamut, matching the Gigabyte, although it wasn’t quite in the same league as the HyperX, which also used an OLED panel. It also didn’t match the HyperX in brightness, but with a peak of 372.4 nits in SDR, the Acer boasts solid brightness for OLED. Our 40% HDR testing revealed up to an impressive 608 nits of brightness.

Acer’s keyboard offers a satisfying feel, with ample throw and cushioning without feeling rubbery. In MonkeyType, I reached 116 words per minute with 99% accuracy on my first try, a great result for me on any laptop. The Acer Key app lets you customize the user-customizable key above the plus key, which is handy for creating one-press access to a favorite app or site.

Though the keyboard doesn't feature per-key backlighting, its four RGB zones can be set to many patterns and colors in the PredatorSense app. When changing lighting settings, the app initially says they’re controlled by Windows Dynamic Lighting, but that isn’t the case since Dynamic Lighting says no compatible devices are detected. But once dismissing the warning in PredatorSense, the app provides intuitive if limited control over the zones and lid logo. Each zone can be assigned a static lighting effect or the entire keyboard set in a dynamic pattern: breathing, neon, wave, ripple, combo, snake, and disco, with customizable speed, direction, and brightness. Settings can be stored in profiles.

Acer also did well with the large touchpad, which offers predictable clicking and my fingers glided smoothly over the matte surface.

Audio performance isn’t a strong point for the Neo 16S AI. Twin speakers embedded in the palm rest barely have enough volume for casual listening, with average clarity and thin bass. In Phil Collins’ “Don’t Lose My Number”, drum hits sounded flat and vocals were somewhat muted.

The speakers are better-suited gaming, offering a decent sense of direction because of their placement on either side of the palm rest, but lack of bass and volume means you'll want a headset. The DTS:X app offers equalizers – without them, the sound is quite tinny. I had the best results leaving the equalizer on Auto.

With ten Torx T6 screws securing the bottom cover, Acer doesn’t make it easy to get inside the Predator Helios Neo 16S AI. Removing the screws is the easy part – finding an entry point for my plastic pry tool provided harder. I eventually found one at the front corners of the chassis by using mild force. The fact that the side vents are part of the bottom cover compounds the difficulty – I needed to find an opening for my pry tool above those as well. The clips made cringeworthy snapping sounds as they were released, though surprisingly, none cracked off.

The effort is not for naught – Acer offers solid upgradeability, with two SODIMM slots under the black plastic cover in the middle, plus an M.2 2280 SSD slot and M.2 2230 wireless card slot. The battery is also replaceable.

Our battery testing consists of web browsing, running OpenGL tests, and streaming videos with the screen at 150 nits while connected to Wi-Fi. The Predator underwhelmed here despite having a large 92 WHr battery – the HyperX laptop outlasted it by almost two hours and the Gigabyte nearly two and a half hours. Most of the blame goes to Acer’s decision to use a screen locked at 240 Hz; more advanced displays would support a variable refresh rate or at least allow it to be lowered to 60 Hz.

We measure laptop surface temperatures during 15 loops of the Metro: Exodus stress test. During the test, the Acer’s temperatures peaked at 103 degrees F between the keyboard G and H keys, 81 F on the touchpad, and a toasty 129 F on bottom-center. Fan noise is audible particularly while gaming; hearing softer sounds in games will require using a headset. For daily use, the laptop hardly makes any noise.

Acer’s 1080p webcam offers a decently sharp picture – I could make out details in my facial hair and eyes from a few feet away. Highlights are also handled reasonably well. It lacks a physical privacy shutter or e-shutter, but it does have infrared (IR) for facial recognition.

PredatorSense remains the prominent app on Acer’s Predator gaming laptops. On the Predator Helios Neo 16S AI, it offers power settings, system monitoring, updates, and system settings. Power profiles include quiet, balanced (the default), performance, and turbo. Performance mode indicates it overclocks the CPU and GPU, but doesn’t specify details, while turbo appears to be performance mode but with the fan speeds maxed.

The app’s settings let you disable Nvidia Optimus, set up optimized battery charging, a bluelight shield, and toggle a 30-second keyboard backlighting timeout. Fan control is possible, though the custom option only allows you to set the RPM, not a curve. Most of the settings above plus lighting effects and a sound equalizer can be saved in scenario profiles.

Acer loads many more apps than PredatorSense – too many: Acer Assist, Acer Chroma Tune, Acer Intelligence Space, Acer LiveArt, Acer documents, Acer Jumpstart, Acer Legal Information, Acer My Key, Acer Purified Voice Console, Acer PurifiedView, and Acer QuickPanel create a lengthy list under the Start Menu’s “A” section. Many of them are simply links to the Windows Store.

Some of the apps actually installed are useful, like Acer My Key mentioned earlier. Others could be: Acer Intelligence Space provides many AI-driven features like a meeting assistant, an image generator, a media search tool, and a game assistant that claims to automatically change the color of your crosshair for maximum visibility. I didn’t test all these out – they aren’t specific to the Predator Helios Neo 16S AI – but it seems that they could add value if you’re not already vested in other AI services.

There’s also a decent amount of bloatware, including a McAfee trial, a Dropbox promotion, and Forge of Empires. While easily uninstalled, they simply don’t belong on a laptop this expensive.

Our Predator Helios Neo 16S AI includes a Core Ultra 9 290HX Plus CPU, Nvidia RTX 5070 Ti graphics card, 16GB of dual-channel RAM, a 1TB PCIe 4.0 solid-state drive, and a 16-inch 2560 x 1600 OLED display with a 240 Hz refresh rate. It retails for $2,774.99 from Best Buy, though I did see it briefly drop to $1,999.99 during my review period.

Best Buy also offers a 32GB version for $3,199.99, but it was discounted to $2,459.99 during our review period, making it less expensive than our model. Pricing is always in flux with the ongoing memory shortages, but Acer’s list prices are in line with market expectations. At Best Buy, many RTX 5070 Ti models hover around the same price as our model and many are pricier.

Acer also lists a $2,099.99 model on its site with 16GB of RAM, a 1TB SSD, and an RTX 5060, though I didn’t find this model for sale.

The Acer Predator Helios Neo 16S AI delivers on most of its thin-and-light gaming promise. Its RTX 5070 Ti provides solid gaming performance on its beautiful 240 Hz OLED display, and it follows up with a comfortable keyboard and touchpad, useful port selection, and solid upgradeability.

The trouble is that its portability is undermined by its below average battery life, an ironic shortcoming for a thin-and-light laptop. Audible gaming fans and lackluster speakers are further compromises, but the limited unplugged runtime is the one that those seeking a more portable package ought to be most concerned about.

But if you’re after a relatively slim gaming laptop to take between places where you can plug in, the Helios Neo 16S AI is an otherwise appealing package.

Charles Jefferies is a freelance reviewer for Tom’s Hardware US. He covers laptop and desktop PCs, especially gaming models.

National Fried Chicken Sandwich Day 2026 deals: Score free food from Burger King, KFC, McDonalds, and more

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

National Sandwich Day took place on Nov. 3, but sandwich lovers have another chance to secure free food this month.

National Fried Chicken Sandwich Day (Nov. 9) is here. We know you've had this date circled in your calendars all year. Forget Thanksgiving and Christmas. This is obviously the date that matters.

A number of popular chains are celebrating this special occasion by launching BOGO offers, limited-edition products, and genuinely free deals. We've scanned the market to bring you a selection of standout promotions.

B-Dubs Rewards members can pick up a free classic or spicy chicken sandwich when they pair it with any $10 food or drink purchase.

Royal Perks members can get a BOGO offer on the elongated Original Chicken Sandwich, or claim a free Royal Crispy Chicken Sandwich with a qualifying digital purchase of $3 to $5.

My Rewards members can get a BOGO $1 Hand-Breaded Chicken Sandwich deal valid across Original, Nashville Hot, or bacon-packed BLT builds.

Jollibee Rewards members unlock an instant BOGO deal across all signature chicken sandwiches.

Place an order of $10 or more on the KFC website or official mobile app to get a free Extra Crispy Chicken Sandwich at checkout.

Make a $5+ minimum order, and you’ll automatically unlock a free Original or Spicy Chik sandwich.

Claim a $2 classic or spicy McCrispy sandwich through the McDonald's app.

Rewards members spending $10 or more online or via the app can snag a free full-size chicken sandwich.

Spend $10 or more on digital orders and enter coupon code SCARYGOOD at checkout to unlock a free chicken sandwich. The offer is active through Nov. 10.

Loyalty members can get a BOGO chicken sandwich reward, available to claim digitally or scan in-store at participating storefronts.

Get a free housemade drink when you order a fried chicken sandwich, plate, or bowl with Katsu.

Urban Bird is running BOGO chicken sandos all day on Nov. 9, for both dine-in guests and digital carryout orders.

Rewards members ordering digitally through the app on Nov. 9 can score a $2 Signature Whatachick’n or Spicy Chicken Sandwich.

Rewards members can grab a free chicken sandwich in any of the chain’s 12 signature dry rubs or sauces with any qualifying menu purchase through Nov. 9.

Wonder is running a BOGO special on Streetbird fried chicken sandwiches across all active regional hubs, accessible directly through the Wonder app.

Use Lyft For Work? How To Get 6%–8% Back, Plus Extra Airline Or Hotel Points

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

I receive compensation for content and many links on this blog. Be aware that websites may earn compensation when a customer clicks on a link, when an application is approved, or when an account is opened. Citibank is an advertising partner of this site, as is American Express, Chase, Capital One, and others. Any opinions expressed in this post are my own, and have not been reviewed, approved, or endorsed by my advertising partners. I do not write about all offers that are available -- instead focusing on miles, points, and cash back (and currencies that can be converted into the same). Terms apply to the offers and benefits listed on this page.

I have a personal preference for Lyft, as regular readers know. One of the things that I really like is that they have the best rewards in rideshare. But I didn’t realize things get even more lucrative for business travelers using a Lyft business profile. They have benefits, like:

keeping all of your regular rewards to use on personal or business rides. earning with Lyft’s airline or hotel partners goes up significantly for eligible business travelers. and pocketing 6% – 8% back in Lyft Credits on business profile rides, to use on your own rides later. That’s why it makes sense to sign up for a business account. Bonus, it lets you separate personal and business rides, which makes expense management easier (and you won’t mistakenly apply personal credits to reimbursable trips as I sometimes do by accident). Earn Lyft Credits On Top of Travel Rewards The original idea of a frequent flyer program was that you’d earn rewards on business travel. Your employer pays for a ticket, you benefit personally with your miles. That kept you loyal. This is really the same idea that the airlines pioneered about 45 years ago. When you ride for work, you can get Lyft Credits to use on your own trips later – 6% for standard rides taken with your business profile and 8% for premium, airport, and scheduled business profile rides. And you can do this even while earning with your preferred travel partner, and you earn those points or miles faster than you do with a personal account (where I’ve already been happy with the points-earning). How To Join I already use Lyft for business travel, so enrolling is a no-brainer since it’s fast and free and you accelerate your earnings. This also lets you bill a business card or directly to your employer and it integrates with popular expense tools. Lyft estimates that over half of business travelers forget to take advantage of automatic company expensing, leading to missed rewards and lack of visibility for organizations. Can We Just Appreciate Rideshare For A Moment? For most of my life there was no such thing as rideshare. For a brief time in Austin where I live, the city government chased it away, and I actually had to re-learn how to get to the airport. Now I pull up my Lyft app and I’m frustrated if I see that my driver is six minutes away. Six minutes. That’s my complaint. A stranger is coming to my exact location, in their personal car, because I touched glass. That’s sorcery with seatbelts. It used to be that you had to stand on the curb with your arm out like the Statue of Liberty, just waving at traffic. “Please! Someone! I have money and a destination!” And the taxis would just drive by. Sometimes they’d slow down just enough to let you experience hope before crushing it. You’d make eye contact with the driver. He’d look at you, look through you, and drive right past. And if it rained? You were done. Some cab would blast through a puddle and soak you like you’re Carrie Bradshaw in Sex and the City. Now you press a button and a tiny map shows you a little car cartoon coming toward you. You get in, you get out. Start to finish, it’s frictionless. And to top it off, you even get rewarded for it. Just by using a business profile, you can earn Lyft Credits and extra travel points or miles on rides your company is already paying for. George Clooney said it best in Up In The Air, “I don’t spend a nickel if I can help it unless it somehow benefits my mileage account.” Your company is spending the nickel. You might as well get the miles. NCT DREAM Takes a Nostalgic Trip With ‘Boogie Woogie’ Video: Exclusive Behind-the-Scenes Photos

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

"It's also packed with unexpected, funny moments," JENO tells Billboard of the visual.

RENJUN, JENO, HAECHAN, JAEMIN, CHENLE and JISUNG were first introduced as NCT DREAM, the colorful and bright teenage unit within NCT 10 years ago, and have since grown into one of K-pop’s leading acts while maintaining their roots musically with themes of youth and self-discovery. The group’s music has continued to evolve alongside the members and their fans, with everything they experienced together over the past decade. Now, following the six members’ contract renewal with label SM Entertainment, NCT DREAM enters a new chapter and celebrates this milestone with its sixth studio album, DREAM AFTER DREAM, which arrived Nov. 9.

The album features lead track “Boogie Woogie,” a song that JENO describes to Billboard as “a celebration of the past 10 years we’ve shared with our fans, and a way for us to all have fun together.” That celebratory spirit and the brotherhood the members have built together come to life in exclusive photos shared with Billboard from the set of the “Boogie Woogie” music video, which was released the same day as the album.

“Boogie Woogie” blends ’70s disco-funk influences with modern synths, capturing the joy of looking back on the past while embracing everything still to come, and its music video reflects that same celebratory spirit. Cowritten by JAEMIN, the track features lyrics that commemorate NCT DREAM’s decade together, expressing gratitude for the memories they’ve shared and how even the simplest moments become meaningful when spent together. The group’s brotherhood is a recurring theme throughout DREAM AFTER DREAM, as shown in the music video for the album’s opening track, “The Sun,” just a few weeks before the album’s arrival.

JENO sums up the “Boogie Woogie” music video in three words: retro, quirky and celebration. “The music video features nostalgic spots for us, like a neighborhood stationery store, a Korean snack bar, a shopping mall and a bowling alley, which really give it that retro feel,” he tells Billboard. “It’s also packed with unexpected, funny moments, like a swing spinning 360 degrees or someone suddenly popping out of a fridge!”

See Billboard‘s exclusive photos from the music video’s set below.

The “Boogie Woogie” music video brings its nostalgic settings to life with the bright and playful energy that NCT DREAM is known for. For JAEMIN, filming in those familiar surroundings was especially meaningful, bringing back memories of his own childhood. He tells Billboard: “Eating tteokbokki and fish cakes on set brought back memories of when we were younger, and for a moment, it felt like I was stepping back in time.”

“It felt like something straight out of a classic teen movie,” recalls JISUNG when discussing the gym and bowling alley scenes. Among the various looks featured throughout the video, he says these as his favorite, particularly for their “sporty and retro hip-hop vibe.”

JENO on the set of NCT DREAM’s “Boogie Woogie” music video.

Some moments on set for “Boogie Woogie” proved to be more amusing than others with the variety of different sets and props. RENJUN, in particular, was challenged while filming at the mall, where he had to walk up a descending escalator. “It was harder than I expected to keep walking against the moving escalator while trying to look natural on camera,” he recalls with a laugh.

When it comes to embodying the concept for “Boogie Woogie,” CHENLE points to HAECHAN. “He’s naturally playful and cheeky, but when he dances, he really knows how to bring out the rhythm and groove,” shares CHENLE. “His energy is a perfect match for the fun, upbeat disco-funk vibe of this song.”

HAECHAN enjoyed exploring the music video’s sets as much as his members, with the supermarket standing out as his favorite. “It looked like a real supermarket and was called Dream Mart,” he shares with a laugh.

“The market was decorated to celebrate its ’10th anniversary’ to match ours, and everything on the shelves — from the water bottles to the snacks — was customized to NCT DREAM,” reveals HAECHAN. “It was fun to look around and discover all the little details. I think our fans will have fun finding them too!”

CHENLE, RENJEN and JISUNG on the set of NCT DREAM’s “Boogie Woogie.”

JENO, JAEMIN and HAECHAN on the set of NCT DREAM’s “Boogie Woogie.”

NCT DREAM on the set of the “Boogie Woogie” music video.

As Matt Damon Comes Off His Big Odyssey Year, He’s Getting Real About The Low Points Of His Career

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Discover the latest entertainment updates and trailers.

Read reviews, interviews and deep dives on new releases.

Get the latest movie, TV and streaming news delivered to your inbox.

Two Scenes In There’s Something About Mary Led To A Six-Month Behind-The-Scenes Battle

I Just Found Out What Christopher Nolan Made Universal’s Head Honcho Do When He Pitched The Odyssey

Digger Took A Big, Bold Swing. John Goodman Thinks It Was ‘Killed’ Before It Even Opened

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Even the brightest stars hit career lows.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Ones of Matt Damon’s 2026 movie releases, The Odyssey, couldn’t stop breaking records after its debut this past July. It became the second highest-grossing movie of the year with a worldwide haul of $1.8 billion. It's also the highest-grossing R-rated movie ever and the 11th highest-grossing movie ever. At present, Damon's name is also being mentioned amid awards season conversations. Still, as the star comes off his big year, he's speaking honestly about what he considers to be the low points of his career.

The Good Will Hunting star was recently asked when he knew The Odyssey would be a hit, and he admitted that moment came when he saw the movie for himself. Decades earlier, Damon had those same instincts, as he explained to GQ. However, in those cases, the results weren't nearly as strong as they are now. While discussing one particular slump, Damon named one of the films he had on the docket in 2000:

The first time was before The Bourne Identity came out. I had done The Legend of Bagger Vance with Will [Smith] and Charlize [Theron], and that movie didn’t do well. And Will was, as he is now, the biggest star. And so it’s like, it’s not Will’s fault. [Laughs]

While The Legend of Bagger Vance may have had one of the best golf scenes in Hollywood history, it was definitely a box office bomb, grossing $39.5 million globally against a reported production budget of $80 million. Not to mention, the sports-drama film has low critics' score of 43% on Rotten Tomatoes. In the long run, it didn't hurt the careers of Damon, Smith or Theron, but I can understand why the Bourne star would've been shaken by the reception at that time.

Damon, unfortunately, experienced another setback after the release of Bagger Vance. There's another failed movie of Damon's that he's still not over, and it was released in 2000 as well:

And then I did All the Pretty Horses with Billy Bob [Thornton], and that was a whole fraught experience. There were different ideas between the studio and between Billy Bob about what the movie should be, and so it ended up getting cut in a way that wasn’t to Billy’s liking and it didn’t do well. And so I had had these misses. And so there was this stretch where the phone just didn’t ring. The silence was…. It was a real rose-colored lenses coming off.

I can imagine how frustrating that must have been to envision what you wanted your movie to be, only for the studio to take over. With that, it's hard not to wonder what could have been if Billy Bob Thornton had had his way. Ultimately, All the Pretty Horses was another bomb for Damon, as it made only $18.1 million worldwide on a budget of $57 million. As for its critics score on Rotten Tomatoes, it's at 33%, as of this writing.

Considering the Saving Private Ryan star's immense success, it's now somewhat wild to think about Damon worrying about his acting career, but it happens. He'd later experience a huge lull with no job offers, until Steven Soderbergh approached him about The Informant! The Ocean’s alum was so happy that he even offered to write the screenplay. While Scott Z. Burns ended up with the screenwriter role, Damon was still honored to be offered this leading role after a long career stretch.

It's so refreshing that Matt Damon has no problem looking back on the rougher points of his career. It’s also not unheard of for actors to look down on their own movies, but Damon speaks more diplomatically about Bagger Vance and All the Pretty Horses. I can imagine setbacks being hard to think about, but I get the sense that they've served as fuel for his career. And just look at him now. He's the lead of one of the biggest movies of all time and could even land himself some awards over the next few months.

On that note, The Odyssey is now available now available to rent on various digital platforms.

Just your average South Floridian cinephile who believes the pen is mightier than the sword.

The AI bubble can wait, but your exit plan can't

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Infrastructure spending outruns measurable returns, with supplier finances key to judging when trouble arrives

The AI bubble isn't about to burst, Forrester reckons, although businesses should prepare for a correction that could upend their suppliers, contracts, and prices.

Spending on infrastructure has raced ahead of measurable returns, the analyst says, yet the financial strength of the companies funding the build-out is what keep Forrester from predicting an imminent crash.

"Fears that an AI bubble is about to burst are premature," said Forrester emerging technology veep Brian Hopkins.

"About nine-tenths of the money building today's AI computing infrastructure comes from large, healthy technology companies paying out of their own cash flow. The risk is concentrated in a few places: a small number of builders under real financial strain, and circular financing deals with the AI labs."

Conveniently, Forrester has developed an AI Bubble Model to help technology leaders monitor the risks and prepare.

Its latest report, "An AI Bubble Burst Is Not Imminent," notes what many in the IT industry, including The Register, have been saying for a while: that the hyperscale operators are committing a record share of revenue into capital expenditure to bring online more infrastructure to support AI development and use.

At the same time, the boost in productivity doesn't justify this supplier capacity build-out. Forrester calculates that the infrastructure investments break even only if AI raises US GDP by 5 percent by 2035. Published estimates suggest productivity improvements could provide about two percentage points of that uplift, leaving roughly $1.3 trillion to come from other sources of value.

A recent report from management consultants Bain & Company came to similar conclusions, estimating the AI industry needs to generate $6 trillion in annual revenue by 2031 to keep funding all the infrastructure it needs, and this will require new uses beyond simply enhancing employee productivity.

Forrester also claims that the forces making AI abundant also make parts of the market unstable, and says that enterprise users should take heed of how prices collapsed as major providers like Anthropic slashed token costs in response to competition from open source models.

If demand disappoints as new capacity comes online, suppliers could cut investment, merge, change prices, or discontinue products. Customers could find themselves rebuilding AI plans around suppliers and services that no longer exist.

Forrester advises businesses to track whether their AI projects deliver measurable value and limit their exposure to vulnerable suppliers – with its help, of course.

Companies can judge how things are going by looking at the gap between suppliers' contracted business and revenue actually recognized, along with how they fund their build-outs. If a vendor leans more on outside investment and customer prepayments, as Oracle and CoreWeave have, this is a red flag to cut back dependencies on them, Forrester says.

Enterprises should also stress-test AI plans against scenarios such as a key vendor announcing end of support in 12 months, and an AI model supplier defaulting on payments to its compute vendor and cutting back service-level agreements for enterprise workloads. Companies should know in advance which AI use cases to drop, what data and workloads need to be moved, and at what cost.

But Hopkins sounded a further note of caution: "Oversupply on its own won't decide whether a correction comes. Demand will, and that's the biggest unknown, because it depends on enterprises finding lasting value in AI beyond simple productivity gains."

Organizations shouldn't pull back, he insists. They should keep investing where AI delivers value that can be demonstrated, closely monitor the financial health of the companies building AI computing, and be ready for corrections.

Recently, the International Monetary Fund started ringing alarm bells that financial markets are increasingly exposed to an AI-driven correction that could lead to widespread economic disruption.

Earlier this year, the Bank for International Settlements also raised concerns that the current situation with the AI industry bore the hallmarks of historical economic bubbles and presented a risk to global economic stability. ®

Tom Hardy's Best <i><em>MobLand </em></i>Scenes So Far, Ranked

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

From Russian roulette to a rare moment of tenderness, Hardy has made Harry da Souza must-watch TV.

The most troubling thing about Harry da Souza is that, in another world, he’d probably be tremendous fun at a party. There are hints. Like when he laughs at Kevin’s joke in season 2 and his face cracks like a dam with one hairline fracture too many. The way he talks to his daughter, Gina, adult to adult, as he finally offers her the truth about his life. There are glimpses, even, of the Harry Jan once fell for, though they grow sparser as MobLand wears on.

But these are glimpses caught only in passing, like a bullet whistling past your ear: you feel the rush of air, you register the sudden, unexpected intimacy of it, and by the time you turn around, it’s already blown some other mug’s face off.

Hardy is a master of that flicker: the split second when the mask slips, the hint of a human who might once have collected stamps or sung in a choir. We’re meant to understand that Harry is a warm-blooded killer, not as cold as his actions imply. He is, as Conrad describes him, a “broken” man, bent out of shape by a life marked by danger. Perhaps that’s what keeps Harry so calm under pressure: a sadness for what his life might have been, and a cold resignation to what it has become.

This is the best scene in the show so far—and it’s tenser than Eddie’s jaw after a night on the sniff.

Harry walks alone into Frankie’s casino, knowing it’s a trap. Frankie, dickie-bowed up to the jugular, is thrown: “You knew it was a trap and you came anyway? Alone… You’ve got something, haven’t you. You rotter. What is it?”

What follows is pure gangster comedy gold: Frankie in all his exuberant camp-Cockney effervescence, Harry calm and giving nothing away.

Suddenly, the scene tightens like a garrotte. All humor departs and Harry reveals he has a hit lined up on Frankie’s son in prison. Frankie spins the revolver and clicks it at Harry’s head—Russian roulette over a literal roulette table. Click, click. Harry never flinches. Then he whips round, seizes the gun and flips the dynamic into total psychological dominance. Finally, Frankie—with no choice but to believe the bluff—lets Harry and Paul go.

In the “Antwerp Blues” episode, mob war finally erupts. Brendan and Seraphina are hostages of the Mexican cartel, and Harry’s job, as always, is to save their bacon.

“Are you ready for this war?” shouts Conrad (Pierce Brosnan) as the pair walk toward the chopper, like Henry V striding toward his charger on St Crispin’s morning.

Harry, a man who won’t raise his voice even over helicopter rotors, offers a chilling four-word reply: “It’s all war, Conrad.”

It captures his weary, pragmatic, ruthless worldview, and it sets up a breakneck sequence in which Harry “MotoGP” da Souza burns up tarmac on a motorbike to reach the warehouse before it’s too late.

“God, that’s sexy,” drools Bella from an upstairs window.

“No, it’s not,” Jan replies desolately. “Not anymore.”

Cast your mind back to episode 1 of season 1, when Harry tracks Hughie “Huey” Campbell to Homerton Hospital after Eddie has stabbed him at a nightclub.

Huey panics as Harry leans over his bed, practically whispering.

“Mate, this is the NHS,” says Harry. “All these poor nurses are run off their feet trying to save people’s lives, which incidentally is what I’m trying to do for you.”

“I’ve just been stabbed, you cunt,” whines Huey.

And so Harry goes full Crocodile Dundee: “That’s not a stab. Eddie doesn’t know how to stab someone.” Then comes the promise that if Huey doesn’t do as he’s told, Harry, or possibly one of his associates, depending on availability, will open him up again and do him serious, serious harm.

I’ve paraphrased some of it for brevity. It’s best watched in full. But the scene perfectly captures the lethal civility of Harry da Souza.

The whole of “Funeral for a Friend” is a masterclass in tension, with everything going sideways at once. For Harry, it’s the first glimpse of his panoptic vision. He is a grandmaster of future possibilities, the Magnus Carlsen of mafiacraft.

When Kevin asks how they’ll get guns into the Stevenson house if the funeral goes tits up, Harry is unbothered: “I’m not worried about that. I’ve got a way in.”

That way in is Freddy, one of Richie’s henchmen, who had a fling with Richie’s wife, Vron (the funeral is hers). Months earlier, Harry lifted Freddy’s phone and cracked the code (123456). Inside, he found sexy pictures. Now he makes the goon say it out loud: “I am the weak link.”

They never even need the guns. The whole scene is there to show how dastardly cunning Harry can be.

I’m folding two scenes into one here, but both involve Gina and take place in the same episode. It starts with Maeve.

Fresh from killing the Medusa to save Gina’s life, Harry confronts Maeve in Majorca. “I have yet to find a line I will not cross in your service,” he growls. Then he leans in. “Well, yesterday I found it. Yesterday you knowingly put my daughter in mortal jeopardy. If you ever put my daughter in a dangerous situation again, I’ll just kill you.”

If we’ve learned anything over the past season and a half, it’s that you don’t threaten Maeve. Remember in season 1, episode 5, when she had Vron Stevenson car-bombed simply for questioning her fuckability in public? Or the fact she sent Eddie on a pointless, quasi-suicide mission to kill a random Irish gangster just to prove a point?

Well, Harry’s threat doesn’t so much ruffle Maeve’s feathers as pluck them right out.

Then comes the quieter scene, and the better one. Harry finally sees what he’s done: dragged his only daughter into the mayhem. The only way to win her back is the truth. “Ask me anything you want about what I do, and I’ll tell you the truth.” Gina asks nothing. She just hugs him. It’s a rare moment of tenderness in MobLand, and a reminder that Harry has a heart, however well he hides it.

Bet25.com Launches Zero Margin Sportsbook to Eliminate Hidden House Edges

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Home » Partners Spotlight » Bet25.com Launches Zero Margin Sportsbook to Eliminate Hidden House Edges

Berlin, Germany, 9th November 2026, PlayNewswire

Every sports bettor deal with the same issue: traditional sportsbooks hide extra profit margins within their odds. Behind flashy ads and complicated bonuses, regular bookmakers quietly lower payouts. When building a parlay or combo bet, these hidden cuts stack up fast

Bet25 is changing this with the Zero Margin Sportsbook, setting the new rules of sports betting. Built on honesty, clear numbers, and fair pricing, Bet25 removes traditional bookmaker tricks, so the real payout is received.

The Problem: How Hidden Margins Hurt Winnings

In a standard sportsbook, the odds you see are not the true odds. The bookmaker trims 5% to 10% off the price of each game to guarantee their own cut before you even place a bet.

On a single match, this might look small. But on combo bets, it becomes a serious problem. Because combo odds multiply together, the hidden house cuts multiply too. Every leg you add gives you a payout that is much lower than the actual math says it should be.

The New Rules: No Hidden House Edge and Naturally Better Odds

The Bet25 Zero Margin Sportsbook is not a temporary promo or a short-lived boost. It is a completely different way to run a sportsbook:

A normal bookmaker takes a cut on pick 1, pick 2, pick 3, and so on. Bet25 charges one single 5% fee whether you pick one game or five.

The difference is easy to see on a multi-leg ticket. Take a simple 5-leg combo where each pick has true 50/50 probability. A regular bookmaker will usually offer around 1.87 on each outcome to keep their cut but zero margin sportsbook will price it by 2.0, again because of no hidden margin inside.

Same five picks. Same $100 bet. Exactly the same risk. But Bet25 pays out $755 more because no hidden cuts were multiplied along the way.

Don’t Just Take the Word for It: Just Compare

Almost every betting site claim to have “the best odds.” Bet25 doesn’t need hype or marketing talk. Instead, the advice is simple: just compare.

Open a betting site side by side with the Bet25 Zero Margin Sportsbook. Check any match, build a 3-leg or 4-leg combo on both, and look at the final payouts. You will see the difference right away. It is not marketing – it is pure math.

Turning a concept this disruptive into reality depended on having the right technological backbone. Bet25 teamed up with Vegangster as its primary platform partner, working side-by-side from initial ideation straight through to full deployment. Vegangster’s flexible, cutting-edge architecture gave the team the technical freedom to execute bold and complex features without friction. In an industry where legacy tech often limits innovation, having a responsive partner who can translate ambitious mechanics into reality has been essential to Bet25’s launch.

Sports betting should be about making smart picks and getting paid fairly when you are right. By creating the new rules of sports betting, removing the hidden house edge, and offering naturally better odds, Bet25 gives players a clear, honest alternative to old-school sportsbooks.

Bet25.com operates under a gaming licence issued by the Government of the Autonomous Island of Anjouan, Union of the Comoros, under Licence No. ALSI-202505001-FI1.

The licence authorizes the company to operate online gaming and wagering services in accordance with the applicable licensing requirements and regulations

Diagrid’s Dapr 1.19 lightens AI agent workflows and drops a control-plane service

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Agent infrastructure startup Diagrid Inc. today released Dapr 1.19 with changes that cut the overhead of the durable workflows behind long-running artificial intelligence agents.

Operators of the open-source runtime can also drop one of its control-plane services. Until 1.19, Dapr’s workflow engine shipped the entire execution history to the worker on every step.

Each one adds to that history. On a job that runs for hours or fans out into dozens of parallel tasks, the payload keeps growing, and Diagrid said that pushed up latency and memory use. Now a worker keeps what it already holds and gets only new events.

That makes the data sent per step independent of how long a workflow has been running. Workers still replay the full history locally. The smaller transfers go only to workers that advertise support for them, which in 1.19 means those built on Dapr’s Java and Python software development kits.

Mark Fussell, Diagrid’s co-founder and chief product officer, said an agent “that runs all afternoon and splits into 50 tasks can’t haul its entire history across the network on every step.”

A second workflow change, an opt-in preview feature called WorkflowsFastPath, stops pushing every workflow event through a durable scheduler reminder. Wake-ups and local activities are handled in place. The reminder path stays as a backstop for recovery, and in Diagrid’s engine benchmarks median step latency fell from 553 milliseconds to 153 milliseconds with the feature on.

Two services have made up Dapr’s control plane until now, and a new mode in 1.19 retires Placement, which distributes the actor placement table. The surviving Scheduler service handles jobs, reminders and workflow scheduling, and it already tracks cluster membership and pushes state to sidecars the same way Placement does.

Yaron Schneider, a co-creator of Dapr who is also Diagrid’s co-founder and chief technology officer, said the pair “matured as two services doing overlapping work.” With the mode on, the Scheduler takes over actor placement and the standalone Placement service is no longer deployed. That spares operators a stateful service and the Raft consensus cluster behind it. Reminders get faster too, because the Scheduler knows which sidecar owns each actor.

Scaling events are also less disruptive. A host joining or leaving used to pause placement across the entire namespace while Dapr re-sent its placement table. In the new mode, only the affected actor types are locked and updated, and the rest keep serving traffic.

New alpha building blocks in 1.19 are aimed at developers of AI applications. A Vector building block offers a portable application programming interface for storing and querying embeddings through similarity search, with Meilisearch as its first supported component.

Binary Store streams large files such as model outputs in chunks so they never have to sit in memory whole. Its first components cover Amazon S3 and the object stores from Microsoft Azure, Google Cloud and Oracle.

Developers working in .NET get Actors.Next, a new actor SDK that generates proxies and registration code at compile time, which cuts runtime overhead and lets actor apps compile with Native AOT.

Dapr 1.19 is available now. WorkflowsFastPath ships turned off, and the Scheduler placement mode is enabled through a Helm setting once every sidecar runs 1.19, with no sidecar restart needed to switch it on or off. Teams running Dapr on Kubernetes can manage the upgrade through Diagrid’s Dapr Ops Dashboard, and the company said the tool typically supports new releases within 24 hours.

Broadcom open-sources its Kubernetes VM manager

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Broadcom Inc. is contributing an open-source project for managing virtual machines with Kubernetes to the Cloud Native Computing Foundation, part of an effort to simplify operations across increasingly diverse enterprise computing environments.

The company announced today that its VM application programming interface project has been accepted into the CNCF Sandbox, the entry point for early-stage cloud-native projects. VM API  provides a vendor-neutral framework for provisioning and managing virtual machines using the same APIs that Kubernetes employs to orchestrate modular and portable software containers.

Broadcom also announced an initiative to standardize application behavior across Kubernetes distributions and expanded support for third-party software on VMware vSphere Kubernetes Service.

The announcements, made at KubeCon North America in Salt Lake City, reflect the growing need for infrastructure that can support traditional applications alongside modern containerized and artificial intelligence workloads.

Although Kubernetes has become the dominant platform for container orchestration, virtual machines remain central to enterprise computing. A Linux Foundation survey of 492 practitioners scheduled to be released today found that VMs account for 53% of production applications, with most respondents expecting that proportion to remain stable. Among organizations expanding their VM deployments, 34% cited AI and agentic workloads as contributing factors.

Broadcom’s VM API project addresses the operational complexity of managing VMs and containers separately. It uses Kubernetes’ declarative management model to create and manage virtual machines across different infrastructure providers while preserving capabilities such as hardware isolation and specialized input/output performance.

“We want to do the same thing now for virtual machines,” said Prashanth Shenoy, Broadcom’s chief marketing officer and vice president of marketing for cloud platform, infrastructure and solutions, referring to Kubernetes’ existing Cluster API initiative.

The goal is to provide consistent management across environments without requiring organizations to abandon their existing virtualization investments.

“Ultimately, our goal is to make virtual machines first-class citizens of a Kubernetes environment without sacrificing what makes them distinct and valuable for our customers,” Shenoy said.

The project could also simplify operations for administrators accustomed to working with Kubernetes rather than maintaining separate management tools for virtualized and containerized infrastructure.

Broadcom’s second initiative addresses a different interoperability problem: inconsistent application behavior across Kubernetes distributions.

The company is co-chairing a newly established Workload Conformance Working Group to develop common expectations for how applications operate across Kubernetes environments.

Existing conformance programs verify that Kubernetes distributions support standard APIs, but that doesn’t guarantee applications will behave identically across platforms. Differences in infrastructure capabilities, configuration and operational practices can introduce incompatibilities.

“Just because two Kubernetes environments are conformant doesn’t necessarily mean that the application will behave consistently across both,” Shenoy said.

The proposed workload conformance standard would help software vendors reduce the need to certify applications separately for individual Kubernetes distributions while giving enterprises greater confidence in application portability.

Broadcom also announced more than 20 newly validated partner solutions for VKS, the Kubernetes runtime integrated into VMware Cloud Foundation.

The validations span observability, security, data management, AI, application delivery, networking and storage. They are intended to help organizations deploy Kubernetes workloads using familiar third-party tools without requiring extensive integration or changes to existing operational practices.

Broadcom said its broader VKS ecosystem now includes more than 90 independent software vendors participating in testing and validation.

AWS commits $100M in AI and cloud credits to health groups in underserved communities

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Amazon Web Services Inc. today committed $100 million worth of artificial intelligence and cloud technology over five years to health organizations working in underserved communities around the world, provided to recipients as AWS credits.

The new AWS Health Impact Initiative is taking aim at a cost problem that AWS says is keeping many of those organizations out of the current wave of AI adoption. AWS points to survey data to explain the focus on credits.

An independent survey of 83 of the more than 400 organizations supported under the company’s earlier AWS Health Equity Initiative found that 60% saw cost as the main barrier to cloud adoption where they operate. Most ranked AWS credits as their highest-priority need. That program began in 2021 with $40 million behind it, and the total had grown to $60 million by the time the last of it was handed out in July 2024.

Timing matters too. A World Economic Forum forecast cited by AWS has generative AI use climbing faster in health care than in any other field. If the organizations closest to underserved patients fall behind on adopting it, AWS argues, health gaps will widen.

Agents and AI are reshaping health services, said Dr. Rowland Illing, chief medical officer and director of health care and life sciences at AWS, and they “too often don’t reach the communities that need them most.” Illing framed the initiative as an extension of the company’s stated aim of democratizing AI and cloud-based health technology.

Any area of health care is eligible, and AWS has singled out three for added weight. The first is access to health services, where AWS wants to support diagnostic testing and stronger health infrastructure in places such as rural areas and low- and middle-income countries.

A second area covers research into women’s and children’s health, with better data and more inclusive studies as the goal. Women’s health draws just 5% of health research funding, the company said, and as many as half the medicines given to children lack pediatric clinical validation.

Healthy food is the third area. AWS said malnutrition among pregnant women, breastfeeding mothers and adolescent girls has climbed 25% since 2020 in the 12 countries hit hardest by the global food and nutrition crisis. Projects there could improve food distribution or predict food security patterns.

Recipients AWS highlighted include women’s health data nonprofit Eve’s Ark, which collects research datasets that are at risk of disappearing when a lab closes or a grant ends. Co-founder and Chief Executive Melissa Antal Iftimie said the group started with a small AWS award and “a single at-risk dataset.” The archive now holds more than a petabyte of women’s health data on AWS, and the group is building a secure research environment where scientists can work with it.

In Haiti, primary care provider Care 2 Communities runs a network of community clinics that has served 350,000 people and handles almost 120,000 patient visits a year. Its electronic medical records sit on AWS, which brings centralized, real-time data to clinics in rural parts of the country. School meals for more than 2.35 million children in India come out of kitchens run by the Akshaya Patra Foundation every school day, and the foundation uses AWS to run those kitchens and get meals out to schools.

Eligibility runs well past health charities to government agencies, research institutions and companies, and AWS will consider a project at any stage up to full production.

Organizations with an AWS account team can submit proposals through it, and a public portal is open to everyone else. Each application has to estimate how many lives the project will improve, and AWS goes through submissions every week. Successful applicants receive credits sized to their own AWS Pricing Calculator estimate of what a project needs, or a starting allocation of up to $10,000. Awardees have 12 months to use the credits and can ask to have that window stretched to 24.

Bitcoin traders face an eight-hour blind spot as Deribit prepares to delay key market data

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Deribit plans to delay public open interest by eight hours from Nov. 10. Prices and trading remain live outside the separate maintenance outage.

Bitcoin traders using Deribit's public positioning data will face an eight-hour lag under a release scheduled for Tuesday, Nov. 10. The change will leave charts of outstanding futures and options positions looking backward even as traders compare them with current prices.

Deribit's updated release notice schedules maintenance for 09:00 UTC, with about 45 minutes of downtime during which clients cannot communicate with the exchange. The release was postponed from Nov. 5.

The broadest change concerns open interest, the positions that remain open. Public figures will show the state eight hours earlier across all futures and options, whether or not any trades were delayed. Deribit says real-time open interest will no longer be available through its API.

A chart combining current Bitcoin prices with that public open interest will mix two different observation times. A price move alongside a displayed rise or fall in positions will therefore be insufficient to establish whether traders added or closed positions during that move.

The same limitation carries into expiry analysis. Concentrations of open interest across option strikes will describe earlier positioning. “Max pain,” the settlement price that would minimize aggregate intrinsic-value payouts to option buyers, will inherit delayed inputs when it uses the affected public data.

Deribit's education material already describes max pain as a limited tool when used alone. The reporting change adds a freshness problem: a dashboard refreshed moments ago can still be displaying positioning from at least eight hours earlier.

On-screen and liquidation trades do not use delayed trade publication. Public open interest still shows positions eight hours earlier.

Relative-time diagram of the scheduled eight-hour open-interest lag. Marks divide that reported interval into eight equal hours; they are not market observations. Selectable trade classes compare reporting rules, not elapsed-time scales.

Block RFQ publication is normally delayed eight hours, shortened to publish before the final settlement hour; final-hour trades publish immediately. No price forecast or live positions are shown.

CryptoSlate's Nov. 5 expiry coverage examined a separate timing gap between options expiry and the US jobs report. The new issue concerns the age of the positioning data itself.

The separate trade-publication feature has a narrower scope than the blanket open-interest lag. It is available on request for qualifying large block request-for-quote (RFQ) trades. On-screen trades, liquidations and blocks outside Block RFQ are excluded from delayed trade publication.

For BTC-settled Block RFQs, the minimum is 1,000 BTC per tranche, with an overall requested trade of at least 3,000 BTC within one settlement cycle.

Qualifying trades will normally be published after eight hours. The wait will shorten where needed so publication is scheduled by 07:00 UTC, one hour before daily settlement. Trades executed in the final hour before the 08:00 UTC settlement will be published immediately.

Volume has its own rule: delayed trades enter 24-hour volume eight hours after execution and remain included for the following 24 hours. Other trades continue to count in real time.

Deribit says execution remains immediate and margin and equity updates remain real time under the reporting rules. After the separately scheduled maintenance outage, the data lag will affect public visibility while trading and risk calculations continue on their own clocks.

Andrew Skurka on How to Prevent and Treat Blisters

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

This article is adapted from Andrew Skurka’s video for The Trek’s YouTube channel, part of a weekly series. It has been edited and condensed for clarity and readability.

Blisters are a fact of life for backpackers, and at their worst, they can totally shut down trips.

I know a thing or two about blisters. I used to be an avid thru-hiker. I’ve walked about 30,000 miles, including the Sea-to-Sea Route (C2C), the Great Western Loop, and the Alaska-Yukon Expedition. For the last 16 years, I’ve also been running a guided backpacking service, so I’ve been taking care of my feet and other people’s feet as well.

When it comes to blisters, an ounce of prevention is worth a pound of cure. Here are five things you can do before leaving home.

Start testing your footwear on low-risk outings. By footwear, I mean both your shoes and your socks. They need to work together for your feet and the conditions in which you’ll be hiking.

Start with a short hike, build up to a full day, and then try a one-night trip. Once you’re confident that a particular combination works, take it on longer backpacking trips.

Once you find shoes and socks that work for you, buy extra pairs if you can. Six months or a year from now, the manufacturer may have changed the fit, fabrics, outsole, or padding.

In my case, I’ve used DeFeet Wooleator socks for 25 years. When I learned they were no longer being made, I bought eight new pairs. I believe those were the only size larges left on the entire internet. That should give me another two or three years before I need to find new socks.

I’ve also been a longtime user of the La Sportiva Bushido. It’s changed a little over time, but it works great for me. That’s one less thing to think about before a trip.

Go get a pedicure if you want, or grab your toenail clippers after a shower. Get rid of the extra length so your toenails don’t bang into the front of your shoes.

The places I most often see calluses are along the side of the big toe and around the rim of the heel. I also see people develop blisters underneath those calluses.

My approach is to carefully remove excess callused skin with a callus remover before a trip. I’m trying to keep that thick patch of skin from moving as one piece. Don’t do this the night before you leave; give your feet time to readjust.

If you know you always get blisters in a particular spot, preemptively tape that area. You’re better off protecting it at home than dealing with a blister once you’re on the trail.

Once you’re in the field, keep up the prevention routine. Get your feet warm and dry at night; I cover this in more detail in 6 Tips for Dry, Healthy Feet.

During the day, take shoeless breaks. If you’re stopping for longer than about 15 minutes, take off your shoes and socks and let some fresh air circulate around your feet.

Inspect your feet regularly, too. I’m often surprised by how many people don’t discover a blister until they get into their tent at night. Their feet already feel tender from hiking all day, so they haven’t noticed a particular problem developing.

Address hot spots as soon as you feel them. A hot spot might feel warm, irritated, or like a pebble in your shoe that stays in the same place.

It’s tempting to think, “Camp is only an hour away. Everyone’s hungry and tired, and I don’t want to interrupt the group. I’ll deal with it there.” But in that hour, a minor hot spot can become a blister.

Do yourself and everyone in your group a favor: stop and take care of it. Once you have a blister, you’re trying to manage something that’s already sore and may get worse.

My blister supplies are part of my broader first aid, foot care, and repair kit.

If you’re a solo thru-hiker, the small scissors on something like a Victorinox Classic can work. If you’re responsible for a group, you’ll want something more substantial. I carry children’s school scissors: not quite trauma shears, but easier to use than tiny multitool scissors.

The cornerstone of my foot care kit is nonstretch Leukotape.

If you don’t need a whole roll, save the backing paper from a sheet of address labels. Run strips of Leukotape along that backing, then cut them out. You can cut patches to size, round the corners, and peel off the backing without touching the adhesive with your fingers.

I avoid wrapping Leukotape around trekking poles or other objects the way people often carry duct tape. In my experience, rolling it onto itself compromises the adhesive. The label backing makes it much easier to handle.

Leukotape is extremely sticky. Once it’s on your feet, it comes off on its own schedule. Be careful removing it, and don’t put the adhesive directly over a blister: it can pull off the blister’s top layer or the fragile skin beneath it.

I carry a balm, such as Badger Balm, to help reduce friction. I also rub a little onto the outside of Leukotape after applying it. The tape’s adhesive can work through the fabric and stick to a sock; the balm helps prevent that.

I don’t use duct tape for foot care very often, but it’s slippery. I sometimes consider it for friction-related problems when I’m not concerned about moisture collecting beneath it.

Gauze covers the blister itself so the tape doesn’t stick directly to it. Tincture of benzoin towelettes help the tape stick when feet are wet, conditions are humid, or the tape is going on a particularly high-stress area.

I carry a needle for sewing and, when I decide to drain a blister, for that purpose as well. Cleaning the instrument beforehand is essential.

The knife has a more drastic use. If someone has a heel blister that won’t get relief, or pressure around the Achilles tendon, I sometimes cut the back of the shoe’s heel cup to reduce that pressure.

This permanently changes the shoe and reduces its support. I make a small cut, have the person walk around, and check for relief before cutting farther. There may be stiff plastic in the heel cup, so cutting it requires care.

I’m not a medical doctor. These are techniques I’ve learned through trial and error in the field, working with actual people and actual feet. Your mileage may vary.

These are the common blister locations I see and how I approach taping each one.

Blisters on the back of the heel are usually associated with friction against the shoe. Cutting the heel cup is an aggressive place to start, so I generally begin with tape.

I cut a strip of Leukotape to fit the hot spot or blister and round the corners so they’re less likely to peel up. A larger area may need two or three strips.

If conditions are wet or I’m concerned about the tape moving, I apply a benzoin towelette where the tape will stick. If there’s already a blister, I cover it with a small piece of gauze before applying the tape.

I use my scissors to help peel the tape from its backing without touching the adhesive. The gauze should cover the blister without sticking out from beneath the tape.

Blisters under a callus on the side of the big toe are common. I’m wary of trimming a callus in the field, so I usually leave it alone and focus on limiting its movement.

A small patch of tape is unlikely to stay put under that much pressure. I typically wrap the toe instead.

Even for my size 11.5 feet, a full-width strip is too wide, so I trim it narrower. I aim to put the overlapping seam on top of the toe, where it’s less likely to peel than underneath.

If the tape bunches around a curve, I make small cuts where it folds so I can press it flat without wrinkles.

I use the same approach as for the big toe: cut a strip long enough to wrap the affected toe, trim it to roughly half the roll’s width, and apply it with the tape lying flat.

These blisters are tough. The skin is thicker here, and there’s often a callus involved. It’s also a high-stress area, so a small piece of tape may peel off as you walk.

I use a larger piece that extends up the sides of the heel toward the ankle, where there’s less pressure working against it. Wherever the tape starts to fold, I cut it so I can press it flat against the skin.

After taping any of these areas, I rub a small dab of balm onto the outside of the tape to help keep it from sticking to my sock.

I’ve only seen this a few times, but a blister on the ball of the foot might be the worst one. I don’t have a great fix. The goal is to take pressure off the blister by creating a donut around it.

I don’t carry moleskin. If I needed to make a donut, one option I would consider is marking the blister’s location on the hiker’s insole, then cutting a corresponding hole in the insole. The idea is for the blister to sit inside that opening, with pressure distributed around it.

If you develop a blister here, think about shortening your days or your trip. It’s a painful spot and difficult to manage once a blister has formed.

I generally lean toward draining blisters because relieving the fluid pressure can make walking less painful. But opening a blister exposes the fragile skin underneath and introduces a risk of infection. As I mentioned earlier, I’m not a medical professional.

When I decide to drain one, I clean the instrument first and consider where fluid is being pushed as the person steps. I want the opening positioned so it can continue to drain.

Afterward, I wash the area with soap and water and leave the skin over the blister in place. Don’t peel that layer off; let it come off on its own.

If the tape comes off easily, I generally remove it and let the foot dry overnight.

If the tape is fresh and removing it would take skin with it, I leave it alone. I still try to get the foot dry, using dedicated sleeping socks or sleeping without socks, depending on the conditions.

Increasing redness around a blister and pain that gets worse each day are warning signs I watch for. If you suspect an infection, get off the trail and seek medical care. Staying on the trail and hoping it improves is not a good option.

Andrew Skurka has backpacked, skied, and packrafted approximately 30,000 miles, including the Sea-to-Sea Route, Great Western Loop, and Alaska-Yukon Expedition. He also runs a guided backpacking program, where much of his foot care experience comes from.

In the video, Andrew says his 2027 guided-trip schedule will be released in November 2026, with registration opening in December. Learn more at andrewskurka.com/guided-trips.

RIP to LIV, the Golf League No One Ever Really Cared About

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

The most remarkable thing about the era of LIV Golf, a five-year run characterized by near-parodic blunders and missteps, is that leadership and its public-facing talent don’t appear to have learned any lessons from their many failures.

From the beginning, the upstart league, established in 2021 as a rival to the dominant PGA Tour, was plagued by its own ineptitude. It’s only fitting—after LIV’s newfound lifeline, private investment firm BC Partners, reneged on a $300 million investment—that the brand’s figureheads are now celebrating the end by taking a victory lap. Tone-deaf doesn’t begin to describe it.

LIV CEO Scott O’Neil—who took over for original boss and Major winner Greg Norman in January of 2025 and was faced with finding new cash flow after Saudi Arabia's Public Investment Fund announced in April that it would no longer finance the operation—clearly exists in a different reality than our own. “Together, our team delivered world-class individual and team competitions across five continents, built a global business, secured pathways to The Open and U.S. Open… and built a league capable of attracting both elite and rising talent from around the world,” he said in a statement late last week. It’s unclear if the farewell tour will continue, though, an even newer report suggests that BC is looking to purchase a bankruptcy loan from LIV’s current owners. If this Hail Mary works, LIV’s future would look much different than the one O’Neil has promised.

Another way to look at it? One of LIV’s biggest accomplishments was that the league, despite its intentions to diversify the game and bring fun back into a sport that had come to personify privilege and exclusivity, inadvertently consolidated the PGA Tour’s power as a monopolistic behemoth now primed to solidify its status as the only league in town. They helped usher in a new era of leadership and investment for the Tour, including the introduction of CEO Brian Rolapp, who is by all accounts the most well-liked and accomplished boss it’s had in quite some time.

LIV also managed to tarnish the reputations of its biggest stars. Jon Rahm, who was vehemently opposed to the dog and pony show when it was announced, only to sheepishly take a bag of blood money so big his great grandchildren will never have to work a day in their lives, is now one of the game’s great “What If?” stories. Prior to the US Open at Brookline Country Club in June 2022, the defending champion was asked about his thoughts on the upstart league. “Money is great,” he said. But Rahm and his wife thought about it further. “Will our lifestyle change if I got $400 million? No, it will not change one bit.” Just over a year later, he defected to LIV.

Then there’s Phil Mickelson, who was poised to become a fixture of the American Ryder Cup team and widely considered a shoo-in to hold a cushy job as an analyst for golf’s biggest broadcasting events. Upon joining the league, he admitted in a conversation with Alan Shipnuck that Saudi Arabia was a terrible bedfellow. (Shipnuck and Mickelson have disagreed over whether the comments were on or off the record.) “They killed Khashoggi and have a horrible record on human rights. They execute people over there for being gay,” he explained. But there was a caveat: LIV was simply a “once-in-a-lifetime opportunity to reshape how the PGA Tour operates.” Miraculously, he got that part right.

Prize money on the PGA Tour is greater than ever, the Tour’s main events have recalibrated, and its season-ending championship series has been completely reimagined. Mickelson won’t be allowed within 100 miles of it.

The league’s biggest attraction, Bryson DeChambeau, is theoretically the worst-situated of the game’s many turncoats, though he’s probably more excited about growing his YouTube channel than he is about playing golf anyway. No one’s put their reputation on the line for this gaggle of buffoons quite like DeChambeau, who spoke proudly about how LIV was “growing the game” and “bringing team golf to the world.” This past weekend, he shared a statement on the dissolution of LIV. “I still believe in team golf,” he wrote. “It may look different in the future, but I still believe it’s essential to growing the game.” A loyal foot soldier, even from the grave.

It was always about the money. Nothing more, nothing less. This was a cash grab of epic proportions, and golfers not good enough to earn their keep on the PGA Tour took a life raft and briefly found themselves in possession of luxury superyachts. Then, low tide left them holding bags of cash and not much else. Was it worth it? For most of these golfers, probably. They were past their primes when LIV began; they’re still past the primes now, and just a helluva lot richer.

The league positioned itself as a disruptor, and disrupted so poorly that they’ve turned the PGA Tour into the strongest version of itself we’ve seen since Tiger Woods was at his peak. All that bloviating about the good of LIV Golf from its loudest talking heads is a bunch of nonsense.

It’s hard to tell if these statements are one last line of defense or more genuine delusion from LIV’s last advocates. They came in with the idea of sticking it to the PGA Tour, which at the time was facing bleak circumstances, bogged down in nonsense before redirecting its energy towards batting this gnat into extinction. Ultimately, LIV helped the PGA Tour save itself.

Now it’s gone for good, and five billion dollars later, we’re not gonna remember a damn thing about it. Not the players, not the leadership, and surely not all the ways they supposedly grew the game while merely growing their own bank accounts.
